◆ This is a plan, not a special mode; I haven’t started implementation. Reply go
  to execute this plan, or tell me what to change.

  Goal

  Execute plans/prompt4.md (Agent A — QSG-T001 resumption): finish the
  independent conservation audit, pass the theorem acceptance gate, and produce
  the minimal formalization of QSG-T001 — covariant conservation of the
  Hadamard-renormalized stress-energy for a free, real, minimally coupled,
  massive scalar field — without repeating completed work.

  Success Criteria

  • All seven audit documents exist under
    research/experiments/qsg-preflight-001/agents/agent-c-conservation-audit/,
    each read back, using only the six dispositions (DERIVED, SOURCE-REPORTED,
    INDEPENDENTLY REPRODUCED, CONDITIONAL, FALSIFIED, BLOCKED; never PASS).
  • The conservation-restoring local correction is derived with explicit
    coefficient and sign under one consistent convention, and the corrected
    tensor's divergence is explicitly shown to vanish (or a BLOCKED obstruction
    is precisely identified).
  • Agent B's defects are documented with exact PDF page/equation locations and
    dispositions.
  • The strongest defensible QSG-T001 proposition is stated with explicit
    assumptions, exclusions, and ambiguities; the Phys-Pseudo representation in
    06-theorem-contract.md uses only the seven permitted constructs.
  • Any Go fixture follows the GR reference architecture; build/test/vet/gofmt
    results are recorded; unsupported analytical steps are never represented as
    executed proofs.
  • Agent B's PDF, completed Agent A reports, and existing audit docs 01/02 are
    untouched.

  Approach

  Resume, don't restart. Verified this session: audit docs 01 (532 lines,
  complete through §8) and 02 (538 lines, complete through §9) already exist and
  are conclusive — 01 fixes Décanini–Folacci as the load-bearing source (Wald
  full texts BLOCKED, abstracts verified) and 02 reproduces the classical
  foundation. Agent A recon (8 docs + G1 closure) is complete, and G1 is closed
  (go.work exists at /home/chaschel/Documents/go/go.work). The missing work is
  docs 03–07, the acceptance gate, and conditional minimal formalization. No
  broad survey, no PDF re-extraction beyond single targeted inspections, no code
  before the gate.

  Key decisions:

  • Trust boundary for 01/02: accept as done after readback verification; do not
    rewrite. Rationale: both end with explicit conclusions and disposition
    tables; prompt4 Rule 1 forbids repeated work.
  • Load-bearing source: Décanini–Folacci (2008) full text, already
    equation-verified in 01; Wald 1977/1978 stay
    SOURCE-REPORTED-at-abstract-level/BLOCKED for details. Rationale: paywalled
    full texts cannot be independently reproduced; DF Eq. (70) already covers
    the needed coefficient.
  • Convention freeze before calculation: DF conventions (signature −+++, □ =
    g^μν∇_μ∇_ν, units ħ=c=1) plus explicit Wightman/symmetric two-point-function
    normalization, frozen in writing at the start of doc 03 work. Rationale:
    prompt4 Rule 5; mixing conventions is the failure mode behind the g·v₁
    coefficient dispute.
  • Formalization location: default to the standalone area
    research/experiments/qsg-preflight-001/theorems/qsg-t001/ per the
    implementation contract (07-implementation-contract.md §1–3), not a new
    directory under shared phys-lib/gr/. Rationale: prompt4 §7 asks for "the
    smallest appropriate new location under phys-lib/gr/", but the binding recon
    contract (§5) forbids theory-specific meaning in shared modules and mandates
    the standalone derivation area; writing into the shared repo also needs
    explicit authorization. Flagged as the one prompt-vs-contract conflict;
    standalone wins unless you direct otherwise.
  • Quantum representation bound: c-number results as pinned goldens +
    per-instance evaluated obligations (Bianchi pattern from
    phys-math/obligation.go); operator algebra, state semantics, and
    renormalization procedure stay human obligations via ProtocolSketch
    UNSUPPORTED. Rationale: feasibility matrix
    (04-qsg-mathematical-feasibility.md) — procedure is NO, result-pinning is
    YES.

  Steps

  1. State inspection and convention freeze. Read back 01/02 fully; confirm
     audit-dir contents; open Agent B's PDF only at pages needed for 03/04 (pp.
     10–13 first, once each); write the frozen convention record (two-point
     normalization, parametrix, operator, curvature conventions) as the preamble
     to doc 03.
  2. Doc 03: Hadamard / point-splitting audit. Derive (□−m²)H explicitly;
     establish the actual smooth source term for W = G⁺−H (falsify or correct
     Agent B's (□−m²)W = 0); audit coincidence-limit derivative handling (total
     vs single-point differentiation); pin subtraction prescription,
     bi-differential operator, parallel transport, coincidence limits.
     Disposition each claim.
  3. Doc 04: conservation and counterterm derivation (critical path). Compute
     the point-split divergence; identify the residual local geometric term;
     derive the correction (candidate ∼ g_μνv₁ — reconcile coefficient/sign
     against DF's general-dimensional formula under the frozen conventions);
     compute its divergence; show explicit cancellation; verify finite-ambiguity
     basis (Tichy–Flanagan vs Salcedo conditionality per 01 §8) and Agent B's
     H⁽¹⁾/H⁽²⁾; keep trace anomaly separate with the corrected formula.
  4. Doc 05: Agent B findings. Build the page-anchored defect table for prompt4
     §4 locations (pp. 1–3, 10–11, 11, 12–13, 13 ×3, 14–16, 16–18, 19–20), each
     with: page/equation, assertion, correct expression or reason
     unestablishable, missing step, minimum correction, disposition. No silent
     repairs.
  5. Doc 06: theorem contract + gate. State the strongest defensible proposition
     with assumptions, exclusions, ambiguities, proof obligations; answer the 10
     acceptance-gate questions explicitly; include the concise Phys-Pseudo
     representation (given/define/assume/claim/derive/if-then-else/? only, per
     02-phys-pseudo-contract.md binding rules).
  6. Doc 07: independent handoff. Final verdict: was Agent B's
     conclusion/derivation correct, what correction is necessary, what was
     reproduced, what remains, readiness for statement-level formalization vs
     executable verification, smallest remaining task. If any gate question
     fails, report BLOCKED with the exact obstruction and stop before Step 7.
  7. Minimal formalization (only if gate passes). Scaffold theorems/qsg-t001/
     per the implementation contract (AGENTS.md, manifest.json,
     research_metadata.json, fixture.go, protocol/derivation_test.go); represent
     established results, ProtocolSketch UNSUPPORTED for analytical steps;
     per-instance witnesses + goldens/anti-goldens + mutation negatives; run
     build/test/vet/gofmt under the workspace recipe (GOWORK + writable
     GOCACHE); record actual results; second-checkout
     phystrace-mcp run_verification if available.
  8. Final handoff report. Answer prompt4 §11's ten questions concisely in chat.

  Non-goals: rewriting 01/02, recon docs, or Agent B's survey; backreaction,
  FLRW, stochastic gravity, noise kernel, Bohmian mechanics; any engine growth,
  parser/validator, new dependencies, or shared-repo edits.

  Validation Plan

  • Per doc (03–07): full readback after writing; disposition-vocabulary check
    (grep for PASS/status words — must be absent); every equation cites frozen
    convention + source equation or derivation step.
  • Gate check: the 10 questions in 06 each answered DERIVED/CONDITIONAL/BLOCKED
    with pointers; no theorem claim stronger than its evidence.
  • Phys-Pseudo check: manual conformance pass against the seven constructs and
    binding rules (names declared before use, non-empty from, ? only for
    unresolved, known-false via refuting derive); no status/provenance
    constructs.
  • Fixture checks (Step 7 only): go build ./..., go test -count=1 ./...
    (fixture + protocol), go vet, gofmt -l under the workspace recipe — all exit
    0/clean with results recorded; manifest strict-decode + unknown-field
    rejection tests; every golden cites its hand derivation; every mutation
    class fails. Highest-risk validation: the Step 3 cancellation calculation
    itself — a plausible final equation is not evidence; the explicit
    divergence-of-correction computation is the gate's load-bearing check.

  Risks / Open Questions

  • Wald full texts BLOCKED (paywalled). Mitigation: already contained — DF is
    the load-bearing source; Wald claims stay SOURCE-REPORTED at abstract level.
    No action unless DF proves insufficient for a needed identity.
  • Correction coefficient/sign reconciliation. The DF general-dimensional
    formula must be rechecked against the frozen two-point normalization; a
    mismatch here is the most likely BLOCKED outcome. Per Rule 10, report it
    early rather than extending the investigation.
  • Prompt-vs-contract location conflict (phys-lib/gr/ vs standalone
    theorems/qsg-t001/). Default: standalone. Open question only if you prefer
    the shared-repo location — that needs your explicit authorization.
  • Session-file naming: prompt4 cites session-c4a1a27d-...(...)1).md; on disk
    it is plans/session-c4a1a27d-d96d-4646-80c2-734cec802d77.md (2.8 MB).
    Treated as the same record; non-blocking.
  • External research: none required for this plan — no new libraries, engines,
    or APIs are recommended; all approach decisions trace to inspected workspace
    evidence (recon docs, 01/02, prompt3/review3, Agent B PDF presence).

  Reply go to execute this plan, or tell me what to change.

  Goal

  Execute Plan 5 (plans/plan5.md) as amended by its adversarial review
  (plans/plan5_review.md): complete audit docs 03–07 under a blind-derivation
  protocol, pass the theorem acceptance gate with an independent cross-check,
  resolve the Go module boundary, and build the minimal QSG-T001 fixture —
  incorporating all five review amendments.

  Success Criteria

  • Blind-derivation rule honored: the residual-divergence calculation is
    recorded and locked (hash + timestamp) before any comparison with the
    expected g·v₁ correction or Décanini–Folacci Eq. (70).
  • All eight convention objects from review §5 are explicitly declared before
    any calculation.
  • Docs 01/02 remain byte-identical (verified by hash), yet every mathematical
    dependency they carry into 03/04 is independently rechecked, not merely
    accepted.
  • The correction passes a fresh-context cross-check that never saw the
    expected answer — or the absence of one is explicitly recorded as a
    limitation.
  • Go packaging is resolved with zero committed-file changes unless a
    documented change is explicitly approved; fixture builds and tests run
    green.
  • SHA-256 before/after hashes exist for the PDF, the eight recon reports, and
    docs 01/02; every validation command has its own recorded exit status;
    gofmt -l output is empty; second-checkout PhysTrace passes or is recorded
    BLOCKED.
  • The two questions — (1) is Agent B's derivation valid, (2) is the theorem
    true under the corrected prescription — are answered separately everywhere.

  Approach

  Follow the review's §8 sequence exactly: integrity baseline → exact convention
  freeze → blind calculation → lock → literature comparison → cross-check → gate
  → contract → packaging preflight → fixture → independent verification →
  handoff. Preserve everything review §7 keeps (frozen recon, frozen 01/02, DF
  as working source, six dispositions, human/math-vs-machine split).

  Key decisions:

  • Blind protocol mechanics: two lock points. Lock 1 after the (□−m²)H /
    residual-source derivation (doc 03 core); Lock 2 after the residual →
    point-split divergence → correction derivation (doc 04 core). Each lock =
    finished prose committed to its doc plus a recorded SHA-256 and timestamp
    line; comparison sections are written only after the corresponding lock.
    Rationale: makes "derive before compare" mechanically checkable instead of
    aspirational.
  • Expected-answer quarantine: during blind stages, the executor works from
    frozen definitions + 01's convention/preamble material only, not from 01
    §8's correction conclusions or Agent B pp. 10–13. Rationale: review's
    critical finding — 01 already discloses the expected correction shape.
  • Provenance posture for 01/02: frozen as records, open as premises — reuse
    for source navigation (equation locations, page refs) but re-derive each
    carried dependency (DF Eq. references used, classical identities,
    coefficient claims) in 03/04 with its own disposition. Rationale: resolves
    the Agent A/C blur without rewriting.
  • Cross-checker form: a fresh-context review pass receiving only model +
    frozen conventions + primary sources, asked to derive/verify the correction
    independently. If run in-session it is a separate agent with quarantined
    context; if unavailable, doc 07 records "no independent cross-review" as a
    limitation and the theorem stays CONDITIONAL on single-agent derivation.
    Rationale: review §4 — don't claim independence you don't have.
  • Packaging preflight placement and default: after the math gate only (no
    premature engineering). Verified facts shaping it: go.work lists exactly the
    five shared modules; equivariance is a package inside the phys-lib module
    (no own go.mod), so a TOE-v6-side theorems/qsg-t001/ cannot join that module
    — the expected smallest arrangement is a new local module plus the
    established disposable-workspace recipe (explicit GOWORK + writable GOCACHE;
    default cache confirmed read-only again this session). Any change to
    committed files (go.work, new go.mod counts as new-file creation in the
    assigned area — allowed; editing go.work — not allowed without approval)
    stops for approval. Rationale: review's second critical finding.
  • PDF extraction: reuse the prior session's extraction (29 extraction markers
    confirmed in the session file) as the index for doc 05; render individual
    pages only where notation is ambiguous. Rationale: review §6A.

  Steps

  1. Integrity baseline. Record SHA-256 of Agent B's PDF, all eight recon
     reports + G1 closure, and docs 01/02; record git status --porcelain for
     affected repos; read back 01/02 fully.
  2. Exact convention freeze. Declare in writing, as the preamble to doc 03:
     G⁺(x,x′), reversed Wightman, symmetric function with normalization (fix the
     factor of two explicitly), exact H(x,x′), W definition, bi-differential
     operator, curvature-sign convention, coincidence-limit derivative
     convention (first- vs second-argument differentiation distinguished).
  3. Blind stage 1 — doc 03. From frozen definitions only: derive (□−m²)H;
     establish W's actual field equation; audit coincidence-limit handling; pin
     subtraction prescription, operator, transport, limits. Lock 1 (hash +
     timestamp), then write the literature-comparison section.
  4. Blind stage 2 — doc 04 core. Propagate the residual through the point-split
     operator: divergence, residual geometric term, correction derivation,
     correction divergence, cancellation — all before opening DF Eq. (70). Lock
     2, then compare against DF/Wald/Hollands-Wald; then finite ambiguities and
     trace separation.
  5. Doc 05 + dependency recheck. Build the page-anchored defect table from the
     prior-extraction index (fresh page renders only for ambiguous notation);
     list each 01/02 dependency carried into 03/04 with its independent recheck
     and disposition.
  6. Cross-check. Run the fresh-context correction review (quarantined context);
     record verdict or record the limitation.
  7. Doc 06. Strongest defensible proposition, exclusions, ambiguities; 10 gate
     questions answered; Phys-Pseudo representation (seven constructs only);
     both review questions answered separately.
  8. Doc 07. Final verdict and readiness decision; on any gate failure, BLOCKED
     with exact obstruction — full stop, no packaging, no fixture.
  9. Packaging preflight (gate passed only). Establish module owner, dependency
     resolution, and smallest build arrangement; verify with a hello-world
     compile probe before writing fixture code; stop for approval if any
     committed-file change is required.
  10. Fixture + validation + handoff. Scaffold theorems/qsg-t001/ per the
      implementation contract; per-command recorded validation (build, tests,
      vet, gofmt-empty); second-checkout phystrace-mcp run_verification
      (phystrace-mcp source confirmed present; if unrunnable, BLOCKED with
      local-vs-independent acceptance distinguished); after-hashes +
      non-modification audit; final ten-question report.

  Non-goals: everything Plan 5 already excludes, plus no go.work edits, no
  shared-module edits, no engine growth, no re-extraction of the full PDF, no
  rewriting 01/02.

  Validation Plan

  • Locks: each lock line contains file path + SHA-256 + timestamp, verifiable
    by re-hashing; comparison sections must postdate their lock.
  • Quote check: blind-stage sections must not cite DF Eq. (70), the g·v₁ shape,
    or Agent B pp. 10–13 conclusions as premises (grep for Eq. (70), v_1, v1 in
    blind sections — must be absent).
  • Per-doc readback; disposition vocabulary (no PASS); gate questions
    individually answered; Phys-Pseudo manual conformance pass.
  • Fixture: each command separately recorded — go build ./...,
    go test -count=1 ./..., go vet ./... exit 0; gofmt -l output empty (not just
    exit 0); manifest/mutation/golden tests per contract; hashes before/after
    identical for all protected files. Highest-risk validation: the blind
    correction derivation and its lock integrity — if the lock is compromised,
    the review's central amendment fails.

  Risks / Open Questions

  • Quarantine leakage. The executor (and this session) already knows the
    expected correction shape. Mitigation: the lock + quote checks above make
    compliance auditable; the fresh-context cross-check is the backstop.
    Residual risk is why the cross-check limitation, if any, must be explicit.
  • Cross-checker independence. A same-session subagent is weaker than a truly
    independent reviewer. Mitigation: quarantined context at minimum; honest
    labeling in 07 regardless.
  • Packaging unknown until Step 9. The new-module + disposable-workspace
    arrangement is expected but unproven; the compile probe in Step 9 de-risks
    it before fixture effort. No open question for you now — approval is
    requested at Step 9 only if committed files must change.
  • PhysTrace second checkout. Facility presence confirmed at source level only;
    runnable status unknown until Step 10. Falls back to recorded BLOCKED, never
    silent optionality.
  • External research: none — no new libraries or methods recommended; every
    decision traces to inspected workspace evidence (review text, plan5, recon
    docs, 01/02, go.work, toolchain probes).

    1 +# 03 — Hadamard and point-splitting audit
    2 +
    3 +Audit: QSG-T001, covariant conservation of the Hadamard-renormalized
    4 +stress-energy tensor. Author: Agent A (resumption, blind protocol per
    5 +`plans/plan5_review.md`). Date: 2026-11-06.
    6 +
    7 +This document reconstructs the quantum Hadamard foundations from the froz
       en
    8 +definitions. Sections marked BLIND are derived without using the expected
    9 +correction, the residual value, or any numbered conservation result from
       the
   10 +literature. Comparison with primary sources follows the recorded locks.
   11 +
   12 +Dispositions follow `01-primary-source-verification.md`:
   13 +DERIVED, SOURCE-REPORTED, INDEPENDENTLY REPRODUCED, CONDITIONAL, FALSIFIE
       D,
   14 +BLOCKED. `PASS` is not used.
   15 +
   16 +---
   17 +
   18 +## 0. Frozen conventions (Step 2 of the execution plan)
   19 +
   20 +Everything in §0 is definition, premise, or standard geometric identity.
       No
   21 +result about the remainder equation, the residual, or the correction is s
       tated
   22 +or assumed here. The quarantine manifest (§0.7) lists what is excluded.
   23 +
   24 +### 0.1 Geometry
   25 +
   26 +- \((M,g)\): four-dimensional, smooth, globally hyperbolic Lorentzian man
       ifold,
   27 +  no boundary. Signature \((-+++)\). Natural units \(c=\hbar=1\).
   28 +- \(\Box_g := g^{\mu\nu}\nabla_\mu\nabla_\nu\), Levi-Civita connection.
   29 +- Real scalar field, mass \(m > 0\), minimal coupling \(\xi = 0\).
   30 +- Fixed classical background; no backreaction.
   31 +- Riemann tensor (Hawking–Ellis slot convention), stated as the definitio
       n
   32 +  from which all commutators below follow:
   33 +  \[
   34 +  R^\rho{}_{\sigma\mu\nu}V^\sigma
   35 +  = (\nabla_\mu\nabla_\nu - \nabla_\nu\nabla_\mu)V^\rho .
   36 +  \tag{0.1}
   37 +  \]
   38 +  Ricci tensor: \(R_{\mu\nu} := R^\rho{}_{\mu\rho\nu}\).
   39 +  Consequences used verbatim later (definitional, no derivation needed):
   40 +  \[
   41 +  [\nabla_\mu,\nabla_\nu]V^\rho = R^\rho{}_{\sigma\mu\nu}V^\sigma,
   42 +  \qquad
   43 +  [\nabla_\mu,\nabla_\nu]\alpha_\rho = -R^\sigma{}_{\rho\mu\nu}\alpha_\si
       gma,
   44 +  \qquad
   45 +  [\nabla_\mu,\nabla_\nu]f = 0
   46 +  \]
   47 +  for vector \(V\), covector \(\alpha\), scalar \(f\).
   48 +
   49 +### 0.2 Two-point functions (objects 1–3, 5)
   50 +
   51 +Work in a geodesically convex neighborhood \(\mathcal C\) of the diagonal
       .
   52 +
   53 +1. **Wightman function.**
   54 +   \[
   55 +   G^+(x,x') := \langle \phi(x)\phi(x') \rangle .
   56 +   \]
   57 +   Premise P1 (free field in a Hadamard state): \(G^+\) satisfies the
   58 +   Klein–Gordon equation in **each** argument:
   59 +   \[
   60 +   (\Box_x - m^2)G^+(x,x') = 0,
   61 +   \qquad
   62 +   (\Box_{x'} - m^2)G^+(x,x') = 0.
   63 +   \tag{0.2}
   64 +   \]
   65 +
   66 +2. **Reversed Wightman function.**
   67 +   \[
   68 +   G^-(x,x') := G^+(x',x) = \langle \phi(x')\phi(x) \rangle .
   69 +   \]
   70 +   By (0.2) applied with arguments exchanged, \(G^-\) also satisfies
   71 +   \((\Box_x - m^2)G^- = 0\) and \((\Box_{x'} - m^2)G^- = 0\).
   72 +
   73 +3. **Symmetric two-point function.** Exact sum, no factor of one-half:
   74 +   \[
   75 +   \boxed{
   76 +   G^{(1)}(x,x') := G^+(x,x') + G^-(x,x')
   77 +   = \langle\{\phi(x),\phi(x')\}\rangle .
   78 +   }
   79 +   \tag{0.3}
   80 +   \]
   81 +   Symmetric: \(G^{(1)}(x,x') = G^{(1)}(x',x)\). From P1, term by term:
   82 +   \[
   83 +   \boxed{
   84 +   (\Box_x - m^2)G^{(1)}(x,x') = 0,
   85 +   \qquad
   86 +   (\Box_{x'} - m^2)G^{(1)}(x,x') = 0.
   87 +   }
   88 +   \tag{0.4}
   89 +   \]
   90 +   Disposition: DERIVED (one line from P1).
   91 +
   92 +4. **Smooth remainder.** With \(H^{(1)}\) the parametrix of §0.3:
   93 +   \[
   94 +   \boxed{
   95 +   S(x,x') := \tfrac12\big[G^{(1)}(x,x') - H^{(1)}(x,x')\big].
   96 +   }
   97 +   \tag{0.5}
   98 +   \]
   99 +   Premise P2 (Hadamard state): \(S\) is smooth (\(C^\infty\); at least \
       (C^4\),
  100 +   the order consumed by the stress-energy operator plus one divergence)
       on
  101 +   \(\mathcal C\). Writing \(H_{\rm sub} := \tfrac12H^{(1)}\), (0.4) give
       s the
  102 +   exact relation
  103 +   \[
  104 +   \boxed{
  105 +   (\Box_x - m^2)S = -(\Box_x - m^2)H_{\rm sub},
  106 +   \qquad
  107 +   (\Box_{x'} - m^2)S = -(\Box_{x'} - m^2)H_{\rm sub}.
  108 +   }
  109 +   \tag{0.6}
  110 +   \]
  111 +   Disposition: DERIVED from P1+P2. The VALUE of the right-hand side is t
       he
  112 +   blind computation of §1; nothing about it is assumed here.
  113 +
  114 +### 0.3 Hadamard parametrix (object 4)
  115 +
  116 +Synge's world function \(\sigma(x,x')\) (half the squared geodesic distan
       ce)
  117 +and the Van Vleck–Morette determinant \(\Delta(x,x')\). Frozen Synge-calc
       ulus
  118 +identities (standard geometric facts, used verbatim):
  119 +\[
  120 +[\sigma]=0,\quad
  121 +[\sigma_{;\mu}]=0,\quad
  122 +[\sigma_{;\mu'}]=0,\quad
  123 +[\sigma_{;\mu\nu}]=g_{\mu\nu},\quad
  124 +[\sigma_{;\mu\nu'}]=-g_{\mu\nu},\quad
  125 +\sigma^{;\mu}\sigma_{;\mu}=2\sigma,
  126 +\]
  127 +\[
  128 +[\Box_x\sigma]=4,\qquad [\Delta^{1/2}]=1,\qquad [\nabla_\mu\Delta^{1/2}]=
       0,
  129 +\tag{0.7}
  130 +\]
  131 +where \([\cdot]\) is the coincidence limit \(x'\to x\) with primed indice
       s
  132 +parallel-transported to \(x\).
  133 +
  134 +Parametrix ansatz at \(D=4\), with explicit prefactor:
  135 +\[
  136 +\boxed{
  137 +H^{(1)}(x,x')
  138 += \frac{1}{8\pi^2}\left[
  139 +\frac{U(x,x')}{\sigma} + V(x,x')\ln|\bar\sigma|
  140 +\right],
  141 +\qquad \bar\sigma := \sigma/\ell^2,
  142 +}
  143 +\tag{0.8}
  144 +\]
  145 +where \(\ell > 0\) is a fixed renormalization length (its dependence is
  146 +tracked as finite ambiguity; nothing below fixes \(\ell\)), and
  147 +\[
  148 +U = \Delta^{1/2}\sum_{n\ge0}u_n\sigma^n,\quad u_0=1,
  149 +\qquad
  150 +V = \sum_{n\ge0}v_n\sigma^n,
  151 +\tag{0.9}
  152 +\]
  153 +are smooth biscalars whose coefficients \(u_n, v_n\) are local curvature
  154 +polynomials fixed by the following defining property DP: the coefficients
        are
  155 +the unique local expressions such that \((\Box_x-m^2)H^{(1)}\) contains n
       o
  156 +\(\sigma^{-2}\), \(\sigma^{-1}\), or \(\ln|\bar\sigma|\) terms (i.e. it i
       s
  157 +smooth on \(\mathcal C\) minus the diagonal, extending smoothly to it).
  158 +
  159 +**No explicit coefficient values are assumed in this freeze.** In particu
       lar
  160 +no explicit \(u_n\) or \(v_n\) appears anywhere in §0. Whatever coefficie
       nt
  161 +values the blind derivation needs are derived there from DP and (0.7).
  162 +
  163 +### 0.4 Bi-differential operator (object 6, with recheck)
  164 +
  165 +Frozen operator, quoted from `02` Eq. (7.1):
  166 +\[
  167 +\boxed{
  168 +\begin{aligned}
  169 +T_{\mu\nu}(x,x')
  170 +={}&
  171 +\frac12\left(
  172 +g_\nu{}^{\nu'}\nabla_\mu\nabla_{\nu'}
  173 ++
  174 +g_\mu{}^{\mu'}\nabla_\nu\nabla_{\mu'}
  175 +\right)\\
  176 +&-\frac12 g_{\mu\nu}
  177 +\left(
  178 +g^{\rho\sigma'}\nabla_\rho\nabla_{\sigma'}+m^2
  179 +\right),
  180 +\end{aligned}}
  181 +\tag{0.10}
  182 +\]
  183 +with \(g_\mu{}^{\mu'}\) the parallel-transport bivector. \(T_{\mu\nu}=T_{
       \nu\mu}\)
  184 +term by term. Recheck V-02-71 (carried-dependency verification, classical
       ,
  185 +non-blind): for \(F(x,x')=\phi(x)\phi(x')\),
  186 +\[
  187 +\nabla_\mu\nabla_{\nu'}F = \nabla_\mu\phi(x)\,\nabla_{\nu'}\phi(x'),
  188 +\]
  189 +and \([g_\nu{}^{\nu'}\nabla_{\nu'}\phi]=\nabla_\nu\phi\), so the first li
       ne of
  190 +(0.10) has coincidence limit
  191 +\(\tfrac12(\nabla_\mu\phi\nabla_\nu\phi+\nabla_\nu\phi\nabla_\mu\phi)
  192 +=\nabla_\mu\phi\nabla_\nu\phi\);
  193 +the second gives
  194 +\(-\tfrac12g_{\mu\nu}(\nabla^\rho\phi\nabla_\rho\phi+m^2\phi^2)\).
  195 +Hence \(\lim_{x'\to x}T_{\mu\nu}(x,x')F\) is exactly the classical (3.4).
  196 +For general symmetric biscalar \(B\), \(\lim T_{\mu\nu}B\) is the same fo
       rm
  197 +evaluated on \(B\)'s coincidence data:
  198 +\([\nabla_\mu\nabla_{\nu'}B]-\tfrac12g_{\mu\nu}
  199 +([\nabla^\rho\nabla_{\rho'}B]+m^2[B])\).
  200 +Disposition: DERIVED (recheck of `02` §7; the only inputs are smoothness
       and
  201 +\([g_\mu{}^{\mu'}]=\delta_\mu^\mu\)).
  202 +
  203 +### 0.5 Subtraction prescription (structural)
  204 +
  205 +\[
  206 +\boxed{
  207 +\langle T_{\mu\nu}\rangle_{\rm raw}
  208 +:= \lim_{x'\to x} T_{\mu\nu}(x,x')\,S(x,x').
  209 +}
  210 +\tag{0.11}
  211 +\]
  212 +The full renormalized tensor is \(\langle T_{\mu\nu}\rangle_{\rm raw}\) p
       lus a
  213 +correction to be derived. **No form of the correction is assumed.**
  214 +
  215 +### 0.6 Derivative and coincidence conventions (objects 7–8)
  216 +
  217 +- \(\nabla_\mu\): unprimed (\(x\)) derivative; \(\nabla_{\mu'}\): primed
  218 +  (\(x'\)) derivative. \(\Box\) acts unprimed unless marked \(\Box'\).
  219 +- \([\cdot]\): coincidence limit with primed indices parallel-transported
       .
  220 +- Synge's rule (frozen coincidence-derivative convention): for any biscal
       ar
  221 +  \(B\),
  222 +  \[
  223 +  \boxed{
  224 +  \nabla_\mu[B] = [\nabla_\mu B + g_\mu{}^{\mu'}\nabla_{\mu'}B],
  225 +  }
  226 +  \tag{0.12}
  227 +  \]
  228 +  and correspondingly for higher derivatives (each total derivative is th
       e
  229 +  coincidence limit of the sum of the unprimed and transported primed
  230 +  derivative). A total derivative of a coincidence limit is NEVER replace
       d by
  231 +  differentiation in one argument alone.
  232 +- Curvature-sign convention: (0.1). All commutators derive from it.
  233 +
  234 +### 0.7 Quarantine manifest
  235 +
  236 +The following appear NOWHERE in §0 and are assumed nowhere in the blind
  237 +sections (§1–§2): any value for \((\Box-m^2)H^{(1)}\), \((\Box-m^2)S\), o
       r
  238 +their coincidence limits; any explicit \(u_n\) or \(v_n\); any residual,
  239 +correction, coefficient, or sign for the conservation argument; any numbe
       red
  240 +conservation equation from any literature source; any content of Agent B
  241 +pp. 10–13. The blind sections cite only §0, premises P1/P2/DP, and their
       own
  242 +previous equations.
  243 +

  252 +
  253 +Lemma L1 (Van Vleck–Morette transport, SOURCE-REPORTED): on \(\mathcal C\
       ),
  254 +\[
  255 +\sigma^{;\mu}\nabla_\mu\Delta^{1/2}
  256 ++ \tfrac12(\Box_x\sigma - 4)\Delta^{1/2} = 0.
  257 +\tag{L1}

  259 +Standard Synge identity; pure background geometry. Findability pointer: a
       ny
  260 +account of the Hadamard construction (e.g. Fulling, Aspects of QFT in Cur
       ved
  261 +Spacetime; Birrell–Davies §6.2). Only the verbatim statement is used.

  265 ++ 2(v_0-[v_0]) + \tau v_0 + 2D_\sigma v_0\),
  266 +where \(\tau := \Box_x\sigma - 4\), \(D_\sigma := \sigma^{;\mu}\nabla_\mu

  267 +and \(v_0\) is the leading coefficient of (0.9) satisfying the standard
  268 +\(v_0\)-transport equation. Then \(M/\sigma\) extends smoothly to the dia
       gonal
  269 +with value \(0\):
  270 +\[
  271 +\lim_{x'\to x} M/\sigma = 0.
  272 +\tag{L2}
  273 +\]
  274 +This is the coincidence content of the \(v_0\)-transport equation. Only t
       he
  275 +verbatim statement is used.

  277 +Geometric data G2 (SOURCE-REPORTED): \([(\Box_x-m^2)\Delta^{1/2}] = R/6 -

       .

  281 +\((\Box-m^2)S\), any divergence, any residual, or any correction.

  283 +### 1.2 The operator applied to the ansatz
  284 +
  285 +For \(f(\sigma)\) smooth away from \(\sigma=0\) and smooth biscalar \(A\)
       ,
  286 +Leibniz plus \(\sigma^{;\mu}\sigma_{;\mu}=2\sigma\) gives
  287 +\[
  288 +\Box_x(fA) = f''(2\sigma)A + f'(\Box_x\sigma)A
  289 ++ 2f'\sigma^{;\mu}\nabla_\mu A + f\Box_x A.
  290 +\tag{1.1}
  291 +\]
  292 +DERIVED. With \(f=1/\sigma\) (\(f'=-1/\sigma^2\), \(f''=2/\sigma^3\)):
  293 +\[
  294 +\boxed{
  295 +\Box_x(U/\sigma)
  296 += \frac{(4-\Box_x\sigma)U - 2\sigma^{;\mu}U_{;\mu}}{\sigma^2}
  297 ++ \frac{\Box_x U}{\sigma}.
  298 +}
  299 +\tag{1.2}
  300 +\]
  301 +With \(f=\ln|\bar\sigma|\) (\(f'=1/\sigma\), \(f''=-1/\sigma^2\); the sca
       le
  302 +\(\ell\) drops out of every derivative):
  303 +\[
  304 +\boxed{
  305 +\Box_x(V\ln|\bar\sigma|)
  306 += \frac{(\Box_x\sigma-2)V + 2\sigma^{;\mu}V_{;\mu}}{\sigma}
  307 ++ (\Box_x V)\ln|\bar\sigma|.
  308 +}
  309 +\tag{1.3}
  310 +\]
  311 +Hence, with \(\tau := \Box_x\sigma-4\),
  312 +\[
  313 +(\Box_x-m^2)H^{(1)}
  314 += \frac{1}{8\pi^2}\left[
  315 +\frac{N_2}{\sigma^2} + \frac{N_1}{\sigma} + N_L\ln|\bar\sigma|
  316 +\right],
  317 +\tag{1.4}
  318 +\]
  319 +\[
  320 +\begin{aligned}
  321 +N_2 &:= -\tau U - 2\sigma^{;\mu}U_{;\mu},\\
  322 +N_1 &:= (\Box_x-m^2)U + (2+\tau)V + 2D_\sigma V,\\
  323 +N_L &:= (\Box_x-m^2)V.
  324 +\end{aligned}
  325 +\tag{1.5}
  326 +\]
  327 +DERIVED from (0.8) and (1.1)–(1.3).
  328 +
  329 +### 1.3 Minimal-\(U\) adoption
  330 +
  331 +Write \(U=\Delta^{1/2}\bar u\) with \(\bar u = 1+u_1\sigma+\cdots\).
  332 +By L1, \(-\tau\Delta^{1/2}-2D_\sigma\Delta^{1/2}=0\) exactly, so
  333 +\(N_2 = -2\Delta^{1/2}D_\sigma\bar u\).
  334 +Adopt the minimal representative \(U := \Delta^{1/2}\) (i.e. \(\bar u\equ
       iv1\)):
  335 +then \(N_2 \equiv 0\). Justification: the leading singularity
  336 +\(\Delta^{1/2}/(8\pi^2\sigma)\) is universal (fixed by the flat-space
  337 +singularity, part of ansatz normalization); the \(\sigma^{-1}\) obstructi
       on
  338 +that \(u_{n\ge1}\) would address is instead absorbed by the \(V\ln|\bar\s
       igma|\)
  339 +term, whose coefficients are then uniquely fixed by DP order by order
  340 +(§1.4–§1.5); two DP-satisfying representatives differ by terms smooth eno
       ugh
  341 +to be absorbed into \(S\). Disposition: DERIVED-choice.
  342 +
  343 +With \(U=\Delta^{1/2}\) and \(V=v_0+v_1\sigma+R_2\)
  344 +(\(R_2=O(\sigma^2)\) smooth):
  345 +\[
  346 +N_1 = (\Box_x-m^2)\Delta^{1/2} + (2+\tau)V + 2D_\sigma V,
  347 +\qquad
  348 +N_L = (\Box_x-m^2)V.
  349 +\tag{1.6}
  350 +\]
  351 +
  352 +### 1.4 Pole cancellation: \([v_0]\)
  353 +
  354 +DP requires \(N_1/\sigma\) smooth, hence \([N_1]=0\) (else a \(\sigma^{-1
       }\)
  355 +pole). Since \([\tau]=[D_\sigma V]=0\):
  356 +\[
  357 +\boxed{
  358 +[v_0] = -\tfrac12[(\Box_x-m^2)\Delta^{1/2}]
  359 += \frac{m^2}{2} - \frac{R}{12}.
  360 +}
  361 +\tag{V0}
  362 +\]
  363 +DERIVED from DP + (1.6) + G2. In particular \([v_0]\) is a local scalar o
       f
  364 +mass dimension 2. No other coefficient value is used or derived here.
  365 +
  366 +### 1.5 Log cancellation: the \([v_1]\) relation
  367 +
  368 +DP requires \(N_L\ln|\bar\sigma|\) bounded, hence \([N_L]=0\) (else a
  369 +logarithmic blow-up). Now
  370 +\((\Box_x-m^2)(v_1\sigma)
  371 += \sigma(\Box_x-m^2)v_1 + 2D_\sigma v_1 + v_1(4+\tau)\),
  372 +whose coincidence value is \(4[v_1]\), while
  373 +\([(\Box_x-m^2)R_2]=0\). Therefore
  374 +\[
  375 +\boxed{
  376 +[N_L] = [(\Box_x-m^2)v_0] + 4[v_1] = 0,
  377 +\qquad\text{i.e.}\quad
  378 +[v_1] = -\tfrac14[(\Box_x-m^2)v_0].
  379 +}
  380 +\tag{V1a}
  381 +\]
  382 +DERIVED from DP + Leibniz. So \([v_1]\) is a local scalar of mass dimensi
       on 4
  383 +(\([v_0]\) has dimension 2, \(\Box-m^2\) adds 2). Its curvature expansion
        is
  384 +NOT derived here and is NOT needed for the conservation argument.
  385 +
  386 +Moreover \(N_L\ln|\bar\sigma|\to0\) at coincidence (\(N_L\) smooth and
  387 +vanishing there, times \(\ln|\bar\sigma|\)).
  388 +
  389 +### 1.6 The remainder limit
  390 +
  391 +Expand \(N_1/\sigma\) using \(V=v_0+v_1\sigma+R_2\):
  392 +\[
  393 +\frac{N_1}{\sigma}
  394 += \frac{M}{\sigma}
  395 ++ 2v_1 + 2D_\sigma v_1 + 4v_1 + o(1),
  396 +\]
  397 +where \(M\) is exactly the combination of L2 and \(o(1)\) collects
  398 +\(R_2\)-terms vanishing at coincidence. The terms: \(2v_1\sigma/\sigma\to
  399 +2[v_1]\); \(2D_\sigma(v_1\sigma)/\sigma = 2D_\sigma v_1 + 4v_1 \to 4[v_1]
       \).
  400 +By L2, \(M/\sigma\to0\). Hence
  401 +\[
  402 +\boxed{
  403 +\lim_{x'\to x} N_1/\sigma = 6[v_1].
  404 +}
  405 +\tag{1.7}
  406 +\]
  407 +DERIVED from (1.6) + V0 + L2.
  408 +
  409 +The smooth source term. From (0.6), (1.4), \(N_2\equiv0\), (1.7), and
  410 +\(N_L\ln|\bar\sigma|\to0\):
  411 +\[
  412 +\boxed{
  413 +(\Box_x-m^2)S = J,\qquad
  414 +J := -\frac{1}{16\pi^2}\left[\frac{N_1}{\sigma}+N_L\ln|\bar\sigma|\right]
  415 +\ \text{smooth},\qquad
  416 +[J] = -\frac{6[v_1]}{16\pi^2} = -\frac{3[v_1]}{8\pi^2}.
  417 +}
  418 +\tag{1.8}
  419 +\]
  420 +DERIVED. By \(x\leftrightarrow x'\) symmetry (\(S\), \(H^{(1)}\) symmetri
       c),
  421 +\((\Box_{x'}-m^2)S\) has the same coincidence limit.
  422 +
  423 +### 1.7 Falsification of the homogeneous equation
  424 +
  425 +Flat-space lemma (DERIVED by Poincaré invariance of the Minkowski Hadamar
       d
  426 +construction): in Minkowski space \(v_0\equiv[v_0]=m^2/2\) is constant, s
       o
  427 +\([(\Box-m^2)v_0] = -m^4/2\) and by (V1a) \([v_1] = m^4/8 \ne 0\) (since
  428 +\(m>0\)). Therefore, in flat space already,
  429 +\[
  430 +[(\Box-m^2)S] = -\frac{3m^4}{64\pi^2} \ne 0.
  431 +\tag{1.9}
  432 +\]
  433 +**Disposition: the assertion that a smooth Hadamard remainder satisfies t
       he
  434 +homogeneous Klein–Gordon equation is FALSIFIED.** Smoothness (\(S\in
  435 +C^\infty\)) does not imply \((\Box-m^2)S=0\); the Minkowski vacuum is an
  436 +explicit counterexample. The remainder obeys the inhomogeneous equation (
       1.8)
  437 +with a generically nonvanishing smooth source.
  438 +
  439 +### 1.8 Lock-1 scope checkpoint
  440 +
  441 +Derived under lock 1 (§0 + L1/L2/G2 + this section): the exact relation
  442 +(0.6); the ansatz calculus (1.2)–(1.5); minimal-\(U\) adoption; (V0); (V1

  449 +## 2. BLIND: smoothness, coincidence limits, and prescription audit
  450 +
  451 +### 2.1 What smoothness gives and does not give
  452 +
  453 +P2 gives: \(S\) smooth, hence (0.11) well-defined; all \(s\)-tensors (§2.
       2)
  454 +exist; \(J\) smooth with limit (1.8). DP gives: \((\Box-m^2)H^{(1)}\) smo
       oth,
  455 +hence (0.6) is an equation between smooth objects. Neither gives a
  456 +homogeneous equation for \(S\): \(H^{(1)}\) is built from local geometry
       to
  457 +match the universal singularity, and the \(\ln|\bar\sigma|\) term forced
       by
  458 +the \(\sigma^{-1}\) obstruction leaves the smooth leftover (1.8). Regular
       ity
  459 +is not dynamics. Disposition: DERIVED (conceptual consequence of §1).
  460 +
  461 +### 2.2 \(s\)-tensor notation and Synge consequences
  462 +
  463 +For the divergence computation, freeze the notation (definitional):
  464 +\[
  465 +s:=[S],\quad s_\mu:=[\nabla_\mu S],\quad
  466 +s_{\mu\nu}:=[\nabla_\mu\nabla_\nu S],\quad
  467 +s_{\mu\nu'}:=[\nabla_\mu\nabla_{\nu'}S]\ (\nu'\ \text{transported}),
  468 +\]
  469 +\[
  470 +t := g^{\mu\nu}s_{\mu\nu'} = [\nabla^\mu\nabla_{\mu'}S].
  471 +\tag{2.1}
  472 +\]
  473 +\(S\) symmetric \(\Rightarrow\) \(s_\mu=[\nabla_{\mu'}S]\),
  474 +\(s_{\mu\nu'}=s_{\nu\mu'}\). From Synge's rule (0.12), DERIVED:
  475 +\[
  476 +\nabla_\mu s = 2s_\mu,
  477 +\qquad
  478 +\nabla_\mu s_\nu = s_{\mu\nu} + s_{\nu\mu'},
  479 +\qquad
  480 +\Box s = [\Box_x S] + t.
  481 +\tag{2.2}
  482 +\]
  483 +The last: \(\Box s = g^{\mu\nu}\nabla_\mu s_\nu
  484 += g^{\mu\nu}s_{\mu\nu} + g^{\mu\nu}s_{\nu\mu'} = [\Box_x S] + t\).
  485 +Every coincidence-limit derivative in the divergence computation cites
  486 +(0.12); total derivatives are never replaced by single-argument derivativ
       es.
  487 +
  488 +### 2.3 Failure-mode analysis (abstract)
  489 +
  490 +Replacing \(\nabla_\mu[\cdot]\) by \([\nabla_\mu\cdot]\) drops the transp
       orted
  491 +primed term (\(s'\)-type halves above): factors of 2 are lost and
  492 +curvature/transport pieces carried by the primed derivative go missing. I
       n a
  493 +divergence computation this corrupts both the \(s\)-transport terms and t
       he
  494 +residual identification. Audit rule enforced in the sequel: each step mov
       ing
  495 +\(\nabla\) across \([\cdot]\) names the Synge instance. Page-anchored
  496 +verification of prior work against this rule is post-lock (doc 05).
  497 +
  498 +### 2.4 Prescription consistency checks
  499 +
  500 +(C1) Well-definedness: (0.11) exists because \(S\) is \(C^4\) by P2 and
  501 +\(T_{\mu\nu}(x,x')\) has smooth coefficients on \(\mathcal C\). DERIVED.
  502 +(C2) Symmetry: \(T_{\mu\nu}=T_{\nu\mu}\) (§0.4) and \(S\) symmetric imply
  503 +\(\langle T_{\mu\nu}\rangle_{\rm raw}\) symmetric. DERIVED.
  504 +(C3) Dimensions: \([S]=2\) (\([G^{(1)}]=[H^{(1)}]=2\)), \(T\) carries
  505 +dimension 2 (two derivatives or \(m^2\)), so \([\langle
  506 +T_{\mu\nu}\rangle_{\rm raw}]=4\). DERIVED.
  507 +Conservation is NOT checked here; it is the blind computation of doc 04.

├ \[gr-qc/0512118\] Hadamard renormalization of the stress-energy tensor for a q
│ uantized scalar field in a general spacetime of arbitrary dimension
│
│ # Hadamard renormalization of the stress-energy tensor
│ for a quantized scalar field in a general spacetime of arbitrary dimension
│
│  Yves Décanini Email: [decanini@univ-corse.fr](mailto:decanini@univ-corse.fr)
│ Affiliation: UMR CNRS 6134 SPE, Equipe Physique Semi-Classique (et) de la Mati
│ ère Condensée,
│ Université de Corse, Faculté des Sciences, BP 52, 20250 Corte, France    Antoi
│ ne Folacci Email: [folacci@univ-corse.fr](mailto:folacci@univ-corse.fr) Affili
│ ation: UMR CNRS 6134 SPE, Equipe Physique Semi-Classique (et) de la Matière Co
│ ndensée,
│ Université de Corse, Faculté des Sciences, BP 52, 20250 Corte, France
│
│ August 8, 2026
│
│ ###### Abstract
│
│ We develop the Hadamard renormalization of the stress-energy tensor for a mass
│ ive scalar field theory defined on a general spacetime of arbitrary dimension.
│  Our formalism could be helpful in treating some aspects of the quantum physic
│ s of extra spatial dimensions. More precisely, for spacetime dimension up to s
│ ix, we explicitly describe the Hadamard renormalization procedure and for spac
│ etime dimension from seven to eleven, we provide the framework permitting the
│ interested reader to perform this procedure explicitly in a given spacetime. W
│ e complete our study (i) by considering the ambiguities of the Hadamard renorm
│ alization of the stress-energy tensor and the corresponding ambiguities for th
│ e trace anomaly, (ii) by providing the expressions of the gravitational counte
│ rterms involved in the renormalization process (iii) by discussing the connect
│ ions between Hadamard renormalization and renormalization in the effective act
│ ion. All our results are expanded on standard bases for Riemann polynomials co
│ nstructed from group theoretical considerations and thus given on irreducible
│ forms.
│
│ ###### pacs
│
│ 04.62.+v, 11.10.Gh
│
│ ## I Introduction
│
│ In semiclassical gravity, spacetime is considered from a classical point of vi
│ ew, i.e. its metric gμ​νg\_{\\mu\\nu} is treated classically, while all the oth
│ er fields propagating on this background (from matter fields to the graviton f
│ ield at one-loop order) are assumed to be quantized. In the last thirty years,
│  this approximation of quantum gravity, usually called quantum field theory in
│  curved spacetime, has permitted us to obtain very interesting results concern
│ ing more particularly i) quantum black hole physics in connection with Hawking
│  radiation, ii) early universe cosmology, iii) the Casimir effect and iv) quan
│ tum violations of classical energy conditions in connection with both the sing
│ ularity theorems of Hawking and Penrose and the existence of traversable wormh
│ oles and time-machines… We refer to the monographs of Birrell and Davies [Birr
│ ell and Davies 1982](#bib.bib1 ""), Fulling [Fulling 1989](#bib.bib2 "") and W
│ ald [Wald 1994](#bib.bib3 "") as well as to references therein for various asp
│ ects of semiclassical gravity. We also refer to a recent review by Ford [Ford
│ arXiv:gr-qc/0504096](#bib.bib4 "") which is a short but rather up to date intr
│ oduction to semiclassical gravity and to its applications. We finally refer to
│  Sec. II.B of Ref. [Flanagan and Wald 1996](#bib.bib5 "") for a very interesti
│ ng critical account about the status and the domain of applicability of semicl
│ assical gravity and to Refs. [Hu and Verdaguer 2001](#bib.bib6 ""); [Hu and Ve
│ rdaguer 2004](#bib.bib7 "") for an extension of semiclassical gravity, the so-
│ called semiclassical stochastic gravity, which also permits us to discuss and
│ investigate its validity.
│

│ si\\rangle, plays a central role in semiclassical gravity. Indeed:

/04-conservation-and-counterterm-derivation.md (+379)
    1 +# 04 — Conservation and counterterm derivation
    2 +
    3 +Audit: QSG-T001. Author: Agent A (resumption, blind protocol per
    4 +`plans/plan5_review.md`). Date: 2026-11-06.
    5 +
    6 +This document derives the divergence of the regularized stress-energy ten
       sor,
    7 +identifies the residual, derives the conservation-restoring correction, s
       hows
    8 +the cancellation, and classifies finite ambiguities. Sections marked BLIN
       D use
    9 +only the locked doc-03 results, the geometric lemmas stated below, and th
       eir
   10 +own equations.
   11 +
   12 +Locked carry-in from doc 03 (all under Lock 1): the remainder equation
   13 +\((\Box_x-m^2)S=J\) with \([J]=-3[v_1]/(8\pi^2)\) (1.8); \([v_0]=m^2/2-R/
       12\)
   14 +(V0); \([v_1]=-\tfrac14[(\Box-m^2)v_0]\) (V1a); \(s\)-tensor notation and
   15 +Synge consequences (2.1)–(2.2); the prescription (0.11) with operator (0.
       10).
   16 +
   17 +Dispositions follow `01`: DERIVED, SOURCE-REPORTED, INDEPENDENTLY
   18 +REPRODUCED, CONDITIONAL, FALSIFIED, BLOCKED. `PASS` is not used.
   19 +
   20 +---
   21 +
   22 +## 1. BLIND: divergence of the regularized tensor
   23 +
   24 +### 1.1 Geometric lemmas (imported standard theory)
   25 +
   26 +Lemma L3 (Ricci identity, DERIVED from doc-03 (0.1)): for any covector
   27 +\(\alpha\),
   28 +\[
   29 +[\nabla^\mu,\nabla_\nu]\alpha_\mu = +R_\nu{}^\sigma\alpha_\sigma .
   30 +\tag{L3}
   31 +\]
   32 +Proof: \([\nabla_\alpha,\nabla_\beta]\alpha_\rho=-R^\sigma{}_{\rho\alpha\
       beta}
   33 +\alpha_\sigma\) (Leibniz on \(V^\rho\alpha_\rho\) with (0.1)), so
   34 +\([\nabla^\mu,\nabla_\nu]\alpha_\mu=-Q^\sigma{}_\nu\alpha_\sigma\) with
   35 +\(Q^\sigma{}_\nu=g^{\mu\lambda}R^\sigma{}_{\mu\lambda\nu}\).
   36 +Now \(Q^\sigma{}_\nu=g^{\sigma\rho}g^{\mu\lambda}R_{\rho\mu\lambda\nu}
   37 +=g^{\sigma\rho}g^{\mu\lambda}R_{\lambda\nu\rho\mu}\) (pair symmetry)
   38 +\(=g^{\sigma\rho}R_{\lambda\nu\rho}{}^\lambda
   39 +=-g^{\sigma\rho}R^\lambda{}_{\nu\lambda\rho}=-R^\sigma{}_\nu\)
   40 +(antisymmetry in the last pair; Ricci \(R_{\nu\rho}=R^\lambda{}_{\nu\lamb
       da
   41 +\rho}\)). Hence the \(+\) sign. In particular
   42 +\([\Box,\nabla_\nu]f=+R_\nu{}^\sigma\nabla_\sigma f\) for scalar \(f\).
   43 +
   44 +Lemma L4 (contracted Bianchi, SOURCE-REPORTED): \(\nabla^\mu R_{\mu\nu}
   45 +=\tfrac12\nabla_\nu R\). Standard; pure background geometry.
   46 +
   47 +Boundary of the import: L3/L4 are background Riemannian geometry with no
   48 +renormalization content.
   49 +
   50 +### 1.2 The raw tensor in \(s\)-tensors
   51 +
   52 +From (0.10)–(0.11), taking the coincidence limit (all primed indices
   53 +transported per §0.6; no differentiated-transport terms arise since limit
       s
   54 +are taken before differentiation):
   55 +\[
   56 +R_{\mu\nu} := \langle T_{\mu\nu}\rangle_{\rm raw}
   57 += s_{\mu\nu'} - \tfrac12g_{\mu\nu}(t + m^2s),
   58 +\tag{1.1}
   59 +\]
   60 +with \(s_{\mu\nu'}=s_{\nu\mu'}\) (symmetry of \(S\)).
   61 +Transport note: the parallel-transport bivector enters only through
   62 +\([\cdot]\); every surviving object is an unprimed tensor at \(x\).
   63 +
   64 +### 1.3 Divergence: reduction to \(s\)-transport
   65 +
   66 +Differentiating (1.1) and using \(\nabla_\nu s=2s_\nu\) (2.2):
   67 +\[
   68 +\nabla^\mu R_{\mu\nu}
   69 += \nabla^\mu s_{\mu\nu'} - \tfrac12\nabla_\nu t - m^2s_\nu .
   70 +\tag{1.2}
   71 +\]
   72 +Lemma T1 (DERIVED): \(\nabla_\nu t
   73 += \nabla^\mu s_{\mu\nu'} + \nabla^\mu s_{\mu\nu} - 2m^2s_\nu - \nabla_\nu
       [J]\).
   74 +Proof: Synge on \(t=[\nabla^\mu\nabla_{\mu'}S]\) gives
   75 +\(\nabla_\nu t=[\nabla_\nu\nabla^\mu\nabla_{\mu'}S]
   76 ++[\nabla_{\nu'}\nabla^\mu\nabla_{\mu'}S]\).
   77 +First bracket: unprimed pair commutes on the primed covector
   78 +(\([\nabla_\nu,\nabla^\mu]\) acts as scalar commutator \(=0\));
   79 +\([\nabla^\mu\nabla_{\mu'}\nabla_\nu S]
   80 +=\nabla^\mu s_{\nu\mu'}-[\nabla^{\mu'}\nabla_{\mu'}\nabla_\nu S]\)
   81 +(Synge)
   82 +\(=\nabla^\mu s_{\nu\mu'}-[\nabla_\nu\Box' S]\)
   83 +(primed/unprimed commute)
   84 +\(=\nabla^\mu s_{\nu\mu'}-m^2s_\nu-\tfrac12\nabla_\nu[J]\)
   85 +(equation \((\Box'-m^2)S=J\); \(J\) symmetric so
   86 +\([\nabla_\nu J]=\tfrac12\nabla_\nu[J]\)).
   87 +Second bracket: \([\nabla^\mu\nabla_{\nu'}\nabla_{\mu'}S]
   88 +=\nabla^\mu s_{\mu\nu}-[\nabla^{\mu'}\nabla_{\nu'}\nabla_{\mu'}S]\)
   89 +(Synge; \([\nabla_{\nu'}\nabla_{\mu'}S]=s_{\mu\nu}\) by symmetry)
   90 +\(=\nabla^\mu s_{\mu\nu}-[\nabla_{\nu'}\Box' S]
   91 +=\nabla^\mu s_{\mu\nu}-m^2s_\nu-\tfrac12\nabla_\nu[J]\).
   92 +Sum, with \(s_{\nu\mu'}=s_{\mu\nu'}\), is T1. ∎
   93 +
   94 +Insert T1 in (1.2) (mass terms cancel):
   95 +\[
   96 +\boxed{
   97 +\nabla^\mu R_{\mu\nu}
   98 += \tfrac12\nabla^\mu s_{\mu\nu'} - \tfrac12\nabla^\mu s_{\mu\nu}
   99 ++ \tfrac12\nabla_\nu[J].
  100 +}
  101 +\tag{D1}
  102 +\]
  103 +DERIVED. Every \(\nabla\)-across-\([\cdot]\) step above cites Synge (0.12
       ).
  104 +
  105 +### 1.4 The transport identity
  106 +
  107 +Lemma TI (DERIVED): \(\nabla^\mu s_{\mu\nu'} = \nabla_\mu s^\mu{}_\nu\).
  108 +Proof: Synge on \(s_{\mu\nu'}=[\nabla_\mu\nabla_{\nu'}S]\):
  109 +\(\nabla^\mu s_{\mu\nu'}=[\Box_x\nabla_{\nu'}S]
  110 ++[\nabla^{\mu'}\nabla_\mu\nabla_{\nu'}S]\).
  111 +First: \([\nabla_{\nu'}\Box_x S]=m^2s_\nu+\tfrac12\nabla_\nu[J]\)
  112 +(commute; equation; \(J\) symmetric).
  113 +Second: \([\nabla_\mu\nabla^{\mu'}\nabla_{\nu'}S]
  114 +=[\nabla_\mu V_{\nu'}]\) with \(V_{\nu'}=\nabla^{\mu'}\nabla_{\nu'}S\);
  115 +Synge gives \(\nabla_\mu[V_{\nu'}]-[\nabla_{\mu'}V_{\nu'}]
  116 +=\nabla_\mu s^\mu{}_\nu-[\Box'\nabla_{\nu'}S]\)
  117 +(\([V_{\nu'}]=s^\mu{}_\nu\) by \(x\leftrightarrow x'\) symmetry)
  118 +\(=\nabla_\mu s^\mu{}_\nu-m^2s_\nu-\tfrac12\nabla_\nu[J]\).
  119 +The \(m^2s_\nu\) and \(\tfrac12\nabla_\nu[J]\) pieces cancel in the sum.
       ∎
  120 +
  121 +Since \(\nabla_\mu s^\mu{}_\nu=\nabla^\mu s_{\mu\nu}\) (metric
  122 +compatibility), TI cancels the \(s\)-transport terms in (D1) exactly.
  123 +
  124 +### 1.5 The residual
  125 +
  126 +From (D1) and TI:
  127 +\[
  128 +\boxed{
  129 +\nabla^\mu\langle T_{\mu\nu}\rangle_{\rm raw}
  130 += \tfrac12\nabla_\nu[J]
  131 += -\frac{3}{16\pi^2}\,\nabla_\nu[v_1].
  132 +}
  133 +\tag{1.3}
  134 +\]
  135 +DERIVED from locked (1.8). The raw point-split divergence is the gradient
  136 +of the local curvature scalar \(-3[v_1]/(32\pi^2)\) (i.e. a gradient of l
       ocal
  137 +geometry, with coefficient fixed by the blind remainder computation).
  138 +Flat-space check: \([J]\) constant \(\Rightarrow\) residual zero, consist
       ent
  139 +with translation invariance (the residual is curvature-driven). The excha
       nge
  140 +of \(\nabla^\mu_x\) with \(\lim_{x'\to x}\) is justified by smoothness on
  141 +\(\mathcal C\) (doc 03, P2).
  142 +
  143 +---
  144 +
  145 +## 2. BLIND: correction and cancellation
  146 +
  147 +### 2.1 The correction
  148 +
  149 +(1.3) is a pure gradient of a local scalar. It is cancelled by a local
  150 +metric term. With the ansatz \(C_{\mu\nu}=K\,g_{\mu\nu}[v_1]\) (\(K\)
  151 +constant; \([v_1]\) the locked local scalar of dimension 4):
  152 +\(\nabla^\mu C_{\mu\nu}=K\,\nabla_\nu[v_1]\) (metric compatibility).
  153 +Cancellation of (1.3) requires \(K=+3/(16\pi^2)\):
  154 +\[
  155 +\boxed{
  156 +C_{\mu\nu} = +\frac{3}{16\pi^2}\,g_{\mu\nu}[v_1],
  157 +\qquad
  158 +\nabla^\mu C_{\mu\nu} = +\frac{3}{16\pi^2}\,\nabla_\nu[v_1].
  159 +}
  160 +\tag{2.1}
  161 +\]
  162 +DERIVED. \(C_{\mu\nu}\) is local, state-independent, geometric, of mass
  163 +dimension 4.
  164 +
  165 +### 2.2 The correction is not independently conserved
  166 +
  167 +From (2.1): \(\nabla^\mu C_{\mu\nu}=+3\nabla_\nu[v_1]/(16\pi^2)\), which
       is
  168 +generically nonzero (it must be: its nonvanishing is what cancels (1.3)).
  169 +**\(C_{\mu\nu}\) is a prescription-restoring term, not a separately
  170 +conserved tensor.** Any description of the conservation-restoring correct
       ion
  171 +as independently conserved while its divergence cancels the residual is
  172 +contradictory. DERIVED (explicit).
  173 +
  174 +### 2.3 Renormalized tensor and conservation
  175 +
  176 +\[
  177 +\boxed{
  178 +\langle T_{\mu\nu}\rangle_{\rm ren}
  179 +:= R_{\mu\nu} + C_{\mu\nu} + \Theta_{\mu\nu},
  180 +\qquad \nabla^\mu\Theta_{\mu\nu}=0,
  181 +}
  182 +\tag{2.2}
  183 +\]
  184 +where \(\Theta_{\mu\nu}\) is the finite conserved ambiguity (§3).
  185 +Theorem (blind conservation): in the frozen model,
  186 +\[
  187 +\boxed{
  188 +\nabla^\mu\langle T_{\mu\nu}\rangle_{\rm ren}
  189 += -\frac{3\nabla_\nu[v_1]}{16\pi^2}
  190 ++ \frac{3\nabla_\nu[v_1]}{16\pi^2} + 0 = 0.
  191 +}
  192 +\tag{2.3}
  193 +\]
  194 +DERIVED. The residual and the correction divergence are computed from the
  195 +same locked remainder equation (1.8); their cancellation is exact in §0
  196 +conventions. No separate conservation of \(C_{\mu\nu}\) is assumed or hol
       ds.
  197 +
  198 +---
  199 +
  200 +## 3. BLIND: ambiguities, scale dependence, and trace
  201 +
  202 +### 3.1 Variation implies conservation
  203 +
  204 +Theorem V (DERIVED, Noether): let \(I[g]=\int_M\sqrt{-g}\,L\) with \(L\)
       a
  205 +local scalar of the metric/curvature, and
  206 +\(H_{\mu\nu}:=(1/\sqrt{-g})\,\delta I/\delta g^{\mu\nu}\).
  207 +Then \(\nabla^\mu H_{\mu\nu}=0\).
  208 +Proof: diffeomorphism invariance, \(\delta_\xi I=0\) for compactly suppor
       ted
  209 +\(\xi\), with \(\delta_\xi g^{\mu\nu}=-\nabla^\mu\xi^\nu-\nabla^\nu\xi^\m
       u\)
  210 +(doc 02 §5.1):
  211 +\(0=\int(\delta I/\delta g^{\mu\nu})\delta_\xi g^{\mu\nu}
  212 +=-2\int\sqrt{-g}\,H_{\mu\nu}\nabla^\mu\xi^\nu
  213 +=+2\int\sqrt{-g}\,(\nabla^\mu H_{\mu\nu})\xi^\nu\)
  214 +(\(H\) symmetric since \(\delta g^{\mu\nu}\) symmetric; boundary dropped)
       .
  215 +\(\xi\) arbitrary \(\Rightarrow\) \(\nabla^\mu H_{\mu\nu}=0\). ∎
  216 +Convention: ambiguity tensors use \((1/\sqrt{-g})\delta/\delta g^{\mu\nu}
       \)
  217 +(no \(-2\)); normalizations are absorbed in coefficients.
  218 +
  219 +### 3.2 Curvature variations
  220 +
  221 +Needed identities: Palatini
  222 +\(\delta R_{\alpha\beta}=\nabla_\lambda\delta\Gamma^\lambda{}_{\alpha\bet
       a}
  223 +-\nabla_\beta\delta\Gamma^\lambda{}_{\alpha\lambda}\)
  224 +(DERIVED in 3 lines from the definition of \(R^\rho{}_{\sigma\mu\nu}\):
  225 +\(\delta\Gamma\) is tensorial, so \(\delta R\) is its antisymmetrized
  226 +covariant derivative); \(\delta g_{\alpha\beta}
  227 +=-g_{\alpha\mu}g_{\beta\nu}\delta g^{\mu\nu}\);
  228 +\(\delta\sqrt{-g}/\sqrt{-g}=+\tfrac12g^{\mu\nu}\delta g_{\mu\nu}\);
  229 +\(g^{\alpha\beta}\delta R_{\alpha\beta}
  230 +=\nabla^\mu\nabla^\nu\delta g_{\mu\nu}-\Box(g^{\mu\nu}\delta g_{\mu\nu})\
       )
  231 +(DERIVED by contracting Palatini with the explicit \(\delta\Gamma\));
  232 +conversion \(\delta/\delta g^{\mu\nu}=-g_{\mu\alpha}g_{\nu\beta}\,
  233 +\delta/\delta g_{\alpha\beta}\).
  234 +
  235 +\(H^{(1)}_{\mu\nu}\) from \(I_1=\int R^2\sqrt{-g}\) (DERIVED):
  236 +\(\delta(R^2)=2R(R_{\alpha\beta}\delta g^{\alpha\beta}
  237 ++g^{\alpha\beta}\delta R_{\alpha\beta})\); integrating by parts twice:
  238 +\[
  239 +\boxed{
  240 +H^{(1)}_{\mu\nu}
  241 += 2RR_{\mu\nu} - \tfrac12g_{\mu\nu}R^2
  242 ++ 2(g_{\mu\nu}\Box - \nabla_\mu\nabla_\nu)R.
  243 +}
  244 +\tag{3.1}
  245 +\]
  246 +Self-check (DERIVED, uses L3 with the verified \(+\) sign and L4):
  247 +\(\nabla^\mu H^{(1)}_{\mu\nu}
  248 +=2R_{\nu\sigma}\nabla^\sigma R+R\nabla_\nu R-R\nabla_\nu R
  249 +-2\nabla_\nu\Box R-2R_\nu{}^\sigma\nabla_\sigma R+2\nabla_\nu\Box R=0\).
       ✓
  250 +
  251 +\(H^{(2)}_{\mu\nu}\) from \(I_2=\int R_{\alpha\beta}R^{\alpha\beta}\sqrt{
       -g}\)
  252 +(DERIVED):
  253 +\(\delta(R_{\alpha\beta}R^{\alpha\beta})
  254 +=2R^{\alpha\beta}\delta R_{\alpha\beta}
  255 ++2R_\alpha{}^\lambda R_{\gamma\lambda}\,\delta g^{\alpha\gamma}\)
  256 +(the second term from \(\delta g^{\cdot\cdot}\) inside \(R^{\alpha\beta}\
       ));
  257 +Palatini + double integration by parts gives, with
  258 +\(M^{\mu\nu}\) the coefficient of \(\delta g_{\mu\nu}\),
  259 +\(M^{\mu\nu}=[\nabla_\alpha\nabla_\nu R^{\alpha\mu}
  260 ++\nabla_\alpha\nabla_\mu R^{\alpha\nu}-\Box R^{\mu\nu}]
  261 +-\tfrac12g^{\mu\nu}\Box R+\tfrac12g^{\mu\nu}R_{\alpha\beta}^2
  262 +-2R^{\mu\lambda}R^\nu{}_\lambda\),
  263 +where the \(-\tfrac12g^{\mu\nu}\Box R\) comes from the
  264 +\(\delta\Gamma^\lambda{}_{\alpha\lambda}\) Palatini piece via contracted
  265 +Bianchi (\(\nabla_\beta R^{\alpha\beta}=\tfrac12\nabla^\alpha R\)).
  266 +Hence, with \(H^{(2)}_{\mu\nu}=-g_{\mu\alpha}g_{\nu\beta}M^{\alpha\beta}\
       ):
  267 +\[
  268 +\boxed{
  269 +\begin{aligned}
  270 +H^{(2)}_{\mu\nu}
  271 +={}&-\nabla^\alpha\nabla_\nu R_{\alpha\mu}
  272 +-\nabla^\alpha\nabla_\mu R_{\alpha\nu}
  273 ++\Box R_{\mu\nu} + \tfrac12g_{\mu\nu}\Box R\\
  274 +&-\tfrac12g_{\mu\nu}R_{\alpha\beta}R^{\alpha\beta}
  275 ++2R_\mu{}^\lambda R_{\nu\lambda}.
  276 +\end{aligned}}
  277 +\tag{3.2}
  278 +\]
  279 +Self-checks: (i) linearized (flat background, keep linear terms):
  280 +\(\partial^\mu H^{(2){\rm lin}}_{\mu\nu}
  281 +=-\tfrac12\partial_\nu\Box R-\tfrac12\Box\partial_\nu R
  282 ++\tfrac12\Box\partial_\nu R+\tfrac12\partial_\nu\Box R=0\)
  283 +(DERIVED, uses linearized Bianchi; validates all derivative-term
  284 +coefficients); (ii) the \(R^2\)-terms
  285 +(\(-\tfrac12gR_{\alpha\beta}^2\) from \(\delta\sqrt{-g}\),
  286 +\(+2R_\mu{}^\lambda R_{\nu\lambda}\) from \(\delta g\) inside
  287 +\(R^{\alpha\beta}\)) are two-line variations; (iii) full nonlinear
  288 +conservation is guaranteed by Theorem V, of which (3.2) is an instance.
  289 +The \(\nabla^\alpha\nabla R\)-form is kept (no Bianchi reshuffle needed).
  290 +
  291 +\(m^4g_{\mu\nu}\) and \(m^2G_{\mu\nu}\) (DERIVED, one line each):
  292 +\((1/\sqrt{-g})\delta\int m^4\sqrt{-g}/\delta g^{\mu\nu}
  293 +=-\tfrac12m^4g_{\mu\nu}\) (absorbing \(-\tfrac12\) in the coefficient,
  294 +basis element \(m^4g_{\mu\nu}\), conserved since \(\nabla g=0\));
  295 +\((1/\sqrt{-g})\delta\int m^2R\sqrt{-g}/\delta g^{\mu\nu}
  296 +=-m^2G_{\mu\nu}\) up to the standard Einstein-tensor variation
  297 +(same computation as (3.1) at linear order in \(R\); conserved by L4).
  298 +
  299 +Gauss–Bonnet (Lovelock lemma, SOURCE-REPORTED): in four dimensions the
  300 +variation of \(\int(R^2-4R_{\alpha\beta}^2+R_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}^2)
  301 +\sqrt{-g}\) vanishes identically, so the \(R_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}^2\
       )
  302 +variation reduces to a combination of (3.1)–(3.2). Standard (Lovelock's
  303 +theorem); pure geometry. \(\Box R\) is a total derivative (no contributio
       n).
  304 +
  305 +### 3.3 Ambiguity basis (polynomial)
  306 +
  307 +Local scalars of mass dimension 4: \(m^4\), \(m^2R\), \(R^2\),
  308 +\(R_{\alpha\beta}^2\), \(R_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}^2\), \(\Box R\).
  309 +Modulo total derivatives and Gauss–Bonnet, every polynomial
  310 +dimension-4 ambiguity is, for dimensionless constants \(c_i\):
  311 +\[
  312 +\boxed{
  313 +\Theta_{\mu\nu}
  314 += c_0\,m^4g_{\mu\nu} + c_1\,m^2G_{\mu\nu}
  315 ++ c_2\,H^{(1)}_{\mu\nu} + c_3\,H^{(2)}_{\mu\nu},
  316 +}
  317 +\tag{3.3}
  318 +\]
  319 +each basis tensor dimension 4, conserved (Theorem V + self-checks).
  320 +DERIVED modulo Lovelock. Scope note: (3.3) is the polynomial basis;
  321 +non-polynomial extensions (e.g. arbitrary functions of \(R/m^2\)) are a
  322 +post-lock literature question, not derived here.
  323 +
  324 +### 3.4 Scale dependence is pure ambiguity
  325 +
  326 +The renormalization length \(\ell\) enters only through
  327 +\(\ln|\bar\sigma|=\ln|\sigma|-\ln\ell^2\) in (0.8). Under
  328 +\(\ell\to\ell'\): \(S\to S+\Delta S\),
  329 +\(\Delta S=(V\ln(\ell'/\ell))/(8\pi^2)\cdot 2\)
  330 +(\(\Delta H^{(1)}=-(1/8\pi^2)V\cdot2\ln(\ell'/\ell)\); \(S=(G^{(1)}-H^{(1
       )})/2\)).
  331 +\(\Delta S\) is smooth, symmetric, local-geometric. Redoing §1.3–§1.5 wit
       h
  332 +\(S\to\Delta S\) (the derivation uses only smoothness, symmetry, and the
  333 +field equation with symmetric source \(J_{\Delta S}=(\Box-m^2)\Delta S\))
       :
  334 +\(\nabla^\mu\lim T_{\mu\nu}\Delta S=\tfrac12\nabla_\nu[J_{\Delta S}]
  335 +=\tfrac12\nabla_\nu[(N_L\cdot{\rm const})]=0\),
  336 +since \([N_L]\equiv0\) at every base point (V1a holds everywhere by DP).
  337 +Hence \(\ell\)-changes shift \(\langle T_{\mu\nu}\rangle_{\rm raw}\) by a
  338 +conserved local geometric tensor — absorbable into \(\Theta_{\mu\nu}\).
  339 +DERIVED. The residual (1.3) and correction (2.1) are \(\ell\)-independent
  340 +(\([v_1]\) carries no \(\ell\)).
  341 +
  342 +### 3.5 Trace
  343 +
  344 +From (1.1): \(g^{\mu\nu}R_{\mu\nu}=t-2(t+m^2s)\), i.e.
  345 +\(\mathrm{tr}_{\rm raw}=-t-2m^2s\).
  346 +With \(t=\Box s-m^2s-[J]\) (from \(\Box s=[\Box_xS]+t\) (2.2) and
  347 +\([\Box_xS]=m^2s+[J]\)):
  348 +\[
  349 +\mathrm{tr}_{\rm raw} = -\Box s - m^2s + [J],
  350 +\qquad
  351 +\mathrm{tr}_{\rm ren} = -\Box s - m^2s + \frac{3[v_1]}{8\pi^2}
  352 ++ \Theta^\mu{}_\mu,
  353 +\tag{3.4}
  354 +\]
  355 +using \(g^{\mu\nu}C_{\mu\nu}=4\cdot3[v_1]/(16\pi^2)\) and locked (1.8).
  356 +DERIVED. The trace is generically nonzero (state pieces \(-\Box s-m^2s\)
  357 +plus geometric \(+3[v_1]/(8\pi^2)\) plus \(\Theta^\mu{}_\mu\)).
  358 +A nonzero trace coexists with exact conservation (2.3): trace and
  359 +divergence are independent projections (the Exhibit is \(C_{\mu\nu}\)
  360 +itself: \(\mathrm{tr}\,C=3[v_1]/(4\pi^2)\ne0\) while \(\nabla C\) cancels
  361 +the residual). No anomaly confusion: in this massive minimal model the
  362 +trace carries state-dependent pieces; the conformal anomaly
  363 +(\(m=0\), \(\xi=1/6\), a different model) is post-lock comparison only.
  364 +
  365 +### 3.6 Lock-2 scope checkpoint
  366 +
  367 +Derived under lock 2 (locked doc-03 items + L3/L4 + this section):
  368 +reduction (D1); transport identity TI; residual (1.3); correction (2.1)
  369 +with non-conservation (2.2); conservation theorem (2.3); Theorem V;
  370 +\(H^{(1)}\) (3.1) with full self-check; \(H^{(2)}\) (3.2) with linearized
  371 +self-check; basis (3.3); scale absorption (§3.4); trace (3.4).
  372 +Assumed from outside: locked doc-03 items, L3 (derived), L4, Palatini
  373 +(derived), Lovelock (SOURCE-REPORTED geometry). Nothing from the
  374 +Feynman-route literature, Agent B, or any expected correction shape is
  375 +used: \(C_{\mu\nu}\) is forced by (1.3), not imported.

  430 +Blind (1.3)/(2.1): \(-3\nabla[v_1]/(16\pi^2)\),
  431 +\(+3g_{\mu\nu}[v_1]/(16\pi^2)\), \(\Theta\) conserved (3.3).
  432 +Structural agreement — INDEPENDENTLY REPRODUCED on both sides: raw
  433 +divergence is a local-curvature gradient; correction is a local scalar
  434 +times the metric; ambiguities are separately conserved; internal
  435 +cancellations are exact in each framework. Coefficient cross-match —
  436 +CONDITIONAL on the smooth framework-difference term \(A\) (doc 03 §3.2):
  437 +the Feynman route (time-ordered, \(i\epsilon\)/averaging) and the symmetr
       ic
  438 +route (\((1/2)G^{(1)}\), minimal subtraction) split "raw vs correction"
  439 +differently by smooth terms; the constraint \([(\Box-m^2)A]=-3[v_1]\) is
  440 +DERIVED from the two verified limits. Exact \(A\) remains a bounded open
  441 +computation; it does not affect (2.3), which is exact in §0 conventions.
  442 +Wald 1978 abstract (verified in `01`): "divergence is the gradient of a
  443 +local curvature term; subtract that term times the metric" — matches
  444 +(1.3)+(2.1) structurally. INDEPENDENTLY REPRODUCED (structure);
  445 +SOURCE-REPORTED (abstract level).
  446 +
  447 +### 4.2 Agent B, pp. 11 and 13 (rendered pages inspected post-lock)
  448 +
  449 +Evidence: `plans/Quantum Stress Energy Conservation Research.pdf` pp. 11,
  450 +13 rendered to PNG and read this session (first inspection of these
  451 +formulas; text extraction blanks equations).
  452 +
  453 +(B1) \(H^{(1)}_{\mu\nu}\) (p. 11): Agent B gives
  454 +\(2\nabla_\mu\nabla_\nu R-2g_{\mu\nu}\Box R+2RR_{\mu\nu}
  455 +-\tfrac12g_{\mu\nu}R^2\).
  456 +Blind (3.1): \(-2\nabla_\mu\nabla_\nu R+2g_{\mu\nu}\Box R+2RR_{\mu\nu}
  457 +-\tfrac12g_{\mu\nu}R^2\).
  458 +The derivative terms have opposite signs. FALSIFIED: moreover
  459 +\(\nabla^\mu(B^{(1)}_{\mu\nu})-\nabla^\mu(H^{(1)}_{\mu\nu})
  460 +=4[\Box,\nabla_\nu]R=+4R_\nu{}^\sigma\nabla_\sigma R\ne0\), so Agent B's
  461 +tensor is not conserved, contradicting its "conserved tensors" label.
  462 +Minimum correction: adopt (3.1).
  463 +(B2) \(H^{(2)}_{\mu\nu}\) (p. 11): Agent B gives
  464 +\(\nabla_\mu\nabla_\nu R-g_{\mu\nu}\Box R-\Box R_{\mu\nu}
  465 ++2R^{\alpha\beta}R_{\alpha\mu\beta\nu}
  466 +-\tfrac12g_{\mu\nu}R_{\alpha\beta}^2\).
  467 +FALSIFIED: at linearized order,
  468 +\(\partial^\mu(B^{(2){\rm lin}}_{\mu\nu})
  469 +=\Box\partial_\nu R-\partial_\nu\Box R-\tfrac12\Box\partial_\nu R
  470 +=-\tfrac12\Box\partial_\nu R\ne0\)
  471 +(linearized Bianchi), while blind (3.2) passes the same check with 0.
  472 +The derivative structure is wrong, not merely unsigned. Minimum
  473 +correction: adopt (3.2).
  474 +(B3) Ambiguity dimensions (p. 11): "\(c_1,c_2,c_3,c_4\) real
  475 +dimensionless" for \(c_1g+c_2G+c_3H^{(1)}+c_4H^{(2)}\).
  476 +FALSIFIED: \([g_{\mu\nu}]=0\) so \([c_1]=4\); \([G_{\mu\nu}]=2\) so
  477 +\([c_2]=2\); only \(c_3,c_4\) are dimensionless. Blind (3.3) carries the
  478 +correct mass factors. (Confirms `01` §6.)
  479 +(B4) "Four axioms" (p. 11): FALSIFIED (Wald 1977 abstract lists five;
  480 +`01` §4.4). Carried, not re-derived.
  481 +(B5) Step-4 homogeneous-\(W\) cancellation (p. 13):
  482 +"\(\Box_x\nabla_{\nu'}W=\nabla_{\nu'}\Box_xW=m^2\nabla_{\nu'}W\)" giving
  483 +\(\tfrac12\lim[g_\nu{}^{\nu'}\Box_x\nabla_{\nu'}W-m^2\nabla_\nu W]=0\).
  484 +FALSIFIED: uses the false homogeneous equation (doc 03 §1.7); the correct
  485 +\(\Box_x\nabla_{\nu'}S=\nabla_{\nu'}\Box_xS
  486 +=m^2\nabla_{\nu'}S+\nabla_{\nu'}J\) leaves \(\tfrac12[\nabla_{\nu'}J]
  487 +=\tfrac14\nabla_\nu[J]\ne0\). Agent B's zero drops the residual source.
  488 +(B6) Residual mechanism (p. 13, step 5): residual attributed to
  489 +"derivatives of parallel transport bivectors \(\nabla^\mu g_\mu{}^{\mu'}\
       )".
  490 +FALSIFIED: \([\nabla g]=0\) at coincidence (smooth transport); had the
  491 +residual come solely from these terms it would vanish. The true residual
  492 +comes from the remainder source \(J\) (1.3). No valid derivation exists.
  493 +(B7) Correction \(C^{(0)}_{\mu\nu}\) (p. 13): called "a local conserved
  494 +curvature tensor" while the proof requires
  495 +\(\nabla^\mu C^{(0)}_{\mu\nu}=+\)(residual) \(\ne0\) to cancel
  496 +\(-\nabla^\mu C^{(0)}_{\mu\nu}\). FALSIFIED (logical contradiction;
  497 +compare blind §2.2, which proves the correction cannot be independently
  498 +conserved). Additionally no explicit formula for \(C^{(0)}\) is ever
  499 +given ("constructed from derivatives of \([V_0]\) and \([V_1]\)" is not a
  500 +derivation): the correction claim is unsubstantiated as stated.
  501 +(B8) Scale shift "\(\propto[V_1]g_{\mu\nu}\)" (p. 11): FALSIFIED as a
  502 +specific form — the shift \(\lim T_{\mu\nu}\Delta S\) involves \(V\)'s

  504 +\(\nabla_\mu\nabla_\nu R\) pieces), not a pure-trace \([V_1]g\) term, and
  505 +no derivation is given. The structural claim (scale shift is a conserved

  510 +\(\langle T^\mu{}_\mu\rangle=(1/16\pi^2)a_2
  511 +=(1/2880\pi^2)[C^2-E_4+\tfrac13\Box R]\).
  512 +FALSIFIED three ways: (i) \(C^2-E_4=2R_{\alpha\beta}^2-\tfrac23R^2\),

│ ## Erratum E1 (post-lock correction, 2026-11-06)
│
│ §2.2's `Box s = [Box_x S] + t` is wrong by a factor of 2. Since
│ \(\nabla_\nu s = 2s_\nu\), \(\Box s = \nabla^\mu(2s_\mu)
│ = 2([\Box_x S] + t)\). Correct formula:
│ \(\boxed{\Box s = 2[\Box_x S] + 2t}\).
│
│ Found by the Step-6 independent cross-check (quarantined agent without
│ audit-directory access; it derived the factor independently and flagged
│ the inconsistency — the cross-check prompt itself contained the error,
│ which the agent caught rather than parroted). Verified by direct
│ recomputation.
│
│ Impact: the \(\Box s\) formula is used only in doc-04 §3.5 (trace (3.4));
│ corrected there by Erratum E2. Docs 03 §§0–1 and 04 §§1–2, §3.1–3.4 use
│ no \(\Box s\) and are unaffected. Lock 1 scope (lines 1–510) is unaltered;
│ this erratum is appended post-lock.

   15 +Frozen model: four-dimensional smooth globally hyperbolic Lorentzian
   16 +\((M,g)\), signature \((-+++)\), \(c=\hbar=1\); real scalar field, mass
   17 +\(m>0\), minimal coupling \(\xi=0\); Hadamard state \(\omega\); fixed
   18 +classical background (no backreaction).

   20 +Definitions (all §0): \(G^{(1)}=G^++G^-\);
   21 +\(H^{(1)}=(1/8\pi^2)[U/\sigma+V\ln|\bar\sigma|]\), \(U=\Delta^{1/2}\);
   22 +\(S=\tfrac12(G^{(1)}-H^{(1)})\); \(T_{\mu\nu}(x,x')\) the symmetrized
   23 +point-split operator (0.10);
   24 +\(R_{\mu\nu}=\lim_{x'\to x}T_{\mu\nu}S\);
   25 +\(C_{\mu\nu}=+3g_{\mu\nu}[v_1]/(16\pi^2)\);

   27 +
   28 +QSG-T001 (conservation): with
   29 +\(\langle T_{\mu\nu}\rangle_{\rm ren}=R_{\mu\nu}+C_{\mu\nu}
   30 ++\Theta_{\mu\nu}\),
   31 +\[
   32 +\boxed{\nabla^\mu\langle T_{\mu\nu}\rangle_{\rm ren}=0.}
   33 +\tag{T}
   34 +\]
   35 +Proof chain (all blind, locked): remainder equation (1.8) \(\to\) residua
       l
   36 +(1.3) \(\to\) correction (2.1) \(\to\) cancellation (2.3). The correction
   37 +is not independently conserved (§2.2); the trace is generically nonzero
   38 +(3.4 as corrected by E2) and compatible with (T).

   57 +  and Palatini (derived); pure background, no renormalization content.

        and V ln terms with pole and log cancellation

       nkowski value -3m^4/(64pi^2) is nonzero for m>0

  // ruleSimplify implements EXPR-SIMPLIFY-001 (§17.4): recursive post-order
  // traversal; at each node, repeatedly apply the closed rewrite list in
  // priority order until locally stable, restarting at the top after each
  // rewrite. Newly formed Add/Mul keep canonical child order. Deterministic
  // and idempotent. Closed list ONLY — no distributivity, factorization,
  // x+x→2x, trig/calculus identities, or theorem proving.

   38 +// StatusSketchUnsupported is the only sketch-step status. There is no
   39 +// supported/executed status: analytical steps are never replayed here.
   40 +const StatusSketchUnsupported = "unsupported"
   41 +
   42 +// SketchStep is one human proof obligation. Deliberately a bare record:
   43 +// there is no Verify or Replay method on QSGSketch anywhere in this
   44 +// module (asserted by TestSketchUnsupported).
   45 +type SketchStep struct {
   46 +    ID        string

   48 +    Evidence  string
   49 +    Status    string
   50 +}
