  577 +\(R_0:=(\Box-m^2)v_0+4v_1+2D_\sigma v_1+\tau v_1\) (from
  578 +\((\Box-m^2)(v_1\sigma)=\sigma(\Box-m^2)v_1+2D_\sigma v_1
  579 ++v_1(4+\tau)\) and \((\Box-m^2)R_2=O(\sigma)\)). DP requires
  580 +\(N_L\ln|\bar\sigma|\) smooth; \([N_L]=0\) ((V1a)) gives only
  581 +\(C^0\) (\(\nabla(R_0\ln)\) diverges as \((\nabla R_0)\ln\) unless
  582 +\(R_0\) vanishes identically). By the same DP+uniqueness argument
  583 +as N2.3, the Hadamard construction satisfies the v1-transport
  584 +equation
  585 +\[
  586 +\boxed{
  587 +R_0\equiv0,\qquad\text{i.e.}\quad
  588 +2D_\sigma v_1+(\tau+4)v_1+(\Box-m^2)v_0=0,
  589 +}
  590 +\tag{N4.3}
  591 +\]
  592 +a first-order linear ODE along geodesics with data (V1a)
  593 +(\([R_0]=[(\Box-m^2)v_0]+4[v_1]=0\)); unique regular solution.
  594 +Corroboration: P2-repaired (\(S\) smooth) forces \(J=(\Box-m^2)S\)
  595 +smooth, hence \(R_0\equiv0\) by consistency (else \(J\notin C^1\)).
  596 +Then \(N_L=\sigma\tilde K^R\), \(N_L\ln|\bar\sigma|=O(\sigma\ln\sigma)\),
  597 +and \([\nabla(N_L\ln|\bar\sigma|)]=[\nabla'(N_L\ln|\bar\sigma|)]=0\)
  598 +(\((\nabla N_L)\ln=O(d\ln d)\to0\) since \([\nabla N_L]=0\);
  599 +\(N_L\nabla\sigma/\sigma=O(d)\to0\)). So with \(N_1/\sigma=\tilde K\)
  600 +(N2.3): \(J=-(8\pi^2)^{-1}[\tilde K+O(\sigma\ln\sigma)]\),
  601 +\([\nabla J]=-(8\pi^2)^{-1}[\nabla\tilde K]\),
  602 +\([\nabla'J]=-(8\pi^2)^{-1}[\nabla'\tilde K]\). \([J]\) (N2.2) is
  603 +unaffected (it used only \([R_0]=0\)). Scope: v1-transport is the
  604 +LAST required order (\(J\in C^1\) suffices for \([\nabla'J]\);
  605 +\(R_1\equiv0\) etc. are not consumed).
  606 +
  607 +### N4.4 F2-weak, F3, and the Q1-split (DERIVED)
  608 +
  609 +F2-weak: \([\nabla v_1]=[\nabla'v_1]\) (\(=w\), so
  610 +\(\nabla[v_1]=2w\)): SOURCE-REPORTED standard Hadamard theory
  611 +(symmetric \(V\)-coefficients; cf. DF §II.2, Christensen 1976/78,
  612 +Brown–Ottewill 1986) — same standing as L1. Only this weak
  613 +(first-derivative-coincidence) form is consumed, not full biscalar
  614 +symmetry. F3: \([\nabla\tau]=[\nabla'\tau]=0\): DERIVED from
  615 +\(\tau=\Box_x\sigma-4=-(1/3)R_{ab}\sigma^{;a}\sigma^{;b}
  616 ++O(\sigma^{3/2})\) (DF (54) contracted; standard), whose every
  617 +first derivative is \(O(d)\to0\). Q1-split (independently verified
  618 +here): with \(Q_1:=\tilde K=6v_1+2D_\sigma v_1+\tau v_1\),
  619 +\([\nabla_\nu D_\sigma v_1]=[\nabla_\nu v_1]=w_\nu\) (via
  620 +\([s^{;a}{}_{;\nu}]=\delta^a_\nu\)),
  621 +\([\nabla_{\nu'}D_\sigma v_1]=-[\nabla_\nu v_1]=-w_\nu\) (via
  622 +\([s^{;a}{}_{;\nu'}]=-\delta^a_\nu\)), so
  623 +\([\nabla Q_1]=8w\), \([\nabla'Q_1]=4w\), \(\nabla[Q_1]=12w\):
  624 +\[
  625 +\boxed{[\nabla'Q_1]=\tfrac13\nabla[Q_1].}
  626 +\tag{N4.4}
  627 +\]
  628 +(F2-weak is necessary: without \(u=u'\) the ratio is
  629 +\((3u'-u)/(3u+3u')\ne\tfrac13\).) Hence
  630 +\([\nabla'J]=\tfrac13\nabla[J]\) and
  631 +\(\delta=\tfrac12\nabla[J]-[\nabla'J]=\tfrac16\nabla[J]
  632 +=-\nabla[v_1]/(8\pi^2)\).
  633 +
  634 +### N4.5 Residual, correction, cancellation, A-data (DERIVED)
  635 +
  636 +From (N4.2), (N4.4), (N2.2):
  637 +\[
  638 +\boxed{
  639 +\nabla^\mu\langle T_{\mu\nu}\rangle_{\rm raw}
  640 +=[\nabla'J]=\tfrac13\nabla[J]
  641 +=-\frac{1}{4\pi^2}\,\nabla_\nu[v_1].
  642 +}
  643 +\tag{N4.5}
  644 +\]
  645 +Forced correction (\(C_{\mu\nu}=Kg_{\mu\nu}[v_1]\),
  646 +\(\nabla C=K\nabla[v_1]\)):
  647 +\[
  648 +\boxed{
  649 +C_{\mu\nu}=+\frac{1}{4\pi^2}\,g_{\mu\nu}[v_1],\qquad
  650 +\nabla^\mu C_{\mu\nu}=+\frac{1}{4\pi^2}\,\nabla_\nu[v_1],
  651 +}
  652 +\tag{N4.6}
  653 +\]
  654 +\[
  655 +\boxed{
  656 +\nabla^\mu\langle T_{\mu\nu}\rangle_{\rm ren}
  657 +=-\frac{\nabla_\nu[v_1]}{4\pi^2}
  658 ++\frac{\nabla_\nu[v_1]}{4\pi^2}+0=0.
  659 +}
  660 +\tag{N4.7}
  661 +\]
  662 +DERIVED — the coefficient \(1/(4\pi^2)\) is forced by the repaired
  663 +residual through the verified \(1/3\) split, not imported from DF
  664 +(no DF number is a derivation input anywhere in N1–N4; DF enters
  665 +only as the N4.1 comparison, which now matches exactly). N4.3/N4.4
  666 +uniqueness layers and non-conservation (N3.3/N3.4) carry over
  667 +verbatim with the new numbers. Trace (N3.6 re-run):
  668 +\(\mathrm{tr}_{\rm ren}=-\tfrac12\Box s-m^2s+[J]
  669 ++4[v_1]/(4\pi^2)+\Theta^\mu{}_\mu\), i.e.
  670 +\[
  671 +\boxed{
  672 +\mathrm{tr}_{\rm ren}
  673 +=-\tfrac12\Box s-m^2s+\frac{[v_1]}{4\pi^2}
  674 ++\Theta^\mu{}_\mu,
  675 +}
  676 +\tag{N4.8}
  677 +\]
  678 +whose geometric piece \([v_1]/(4\pi^2)\) formally reproduces DF
  679 +(111) under conformal \([v_1]\) — independent corroboration.
  680 +A-data: \(W=8\pi^2S+A\) (definitional; \(A\) smooth symmetric)
  681 +gives \([(\Box-m^2)A]=-6v_1-8\pi^2[J]=0\) (DERIVED from N2.2 +
  682 +DF-(55)) and \([\nabla'J_A]=[\nabla'J_W]-8\pi^2[\nabla'J_S]
  683 +=-2\nabla v_1-8\pi^2(-\nabla[v_1]/(4\pi^2))=0\) (DERIVED-combination
  684 +from DF's \(\nabla\lim\mathcal TW=-2\nabla v_1\) via the structural
  685 +(N4.2) applied to \(W\)); hence \(\nabla\lim TA=[\nabla'J_A]=0\):
  686 +\(A\) contributes a conserved local tensor (absorbed into
  687 +\(\Theta\)). Explicit \(A\): still open (O2 refined — blocks only
  688 +full framework identification, not the internal theorem).
  689 +

   21 +## 1. Theorem (strongest defensible proposition)
   22 +
   23 +Frozen model: four-dimensional smooth globally hyperbolic Lorentzian
   24 +\((M,g)\), signature \((-+++)\), \(c=\hbar=1\); real scalar field, mass
   25 +\(m>0\), minimal coupling \(\xi=0\); Hadamard state \(\omega\); fixed
   26 +classical background (no backreaction).
   27 +
   28 +Definitions (doc 08 N1): \(G^{(1)}=G^++G^-\);
   29 +\(H^{(1)}=(1/8\pi^2)[U/\sigma+V\ln|\bar\sigma|]\), \(U=\Delta^{1/2}\);
   30 +\(S=\tfrac12G^{(1)}-H^{(1)}\) (Alternative A, N1.6);
   31 +\(T_{\mu\nu}(x,x')\) the symmetrized point-split operator (0.10);
   32 +\(R_{\mu\nu}=\lim_{x'\to x}T_{\mu\nu}S\);
   33 +\(C_{\mu\nu}=+g_{\mu\nu}[v_1]/(4\pi^2)\) (N4.6);

  150 +## 6. Phys-Pseudo representation (conforming, revised)
  151 +
  152 +Checked against PSEUDO.md via the recon contract: seven constructs
  153 +only; `define` with `:=`; `derive` with non-empty `from` + `|`;
  154 +no argument lists; no nesting; multiline `if`; prose-only `?`;
  155 +no status words; no claim promotion. `?` marks exactly the open
  156 +obligations O2-refined and O3 (O1 closed, O4–O5 access/future-work
  157 +items stated in prose above). Premise lists are complete per
  158 +review §5 (no omitted-premise hiding: every transport/Synge/
  159 +curvature/J-premise is named).
  160 +
  161 +```
  162 +given MODEL: four-dimensional smooth globally hyperbolic Lorentzian manif
       old signature (-+++) units c=hbar=1
  163 +given FIELD: real scalar field mass m>0 minimal coupling xi=0 fixed class
       ical background no backreaction
  164 +given STATE: Hadamard state with two-point function satisfying Klein-Gord
       on in each argument
  165 +define SYMM2PT := sum of Wightman function and reversed Wightman function
  166 +define PARAMETRIX := Hadamard singular part with U=Delta^{1/2} and V seri
       es
  167 +define REMAINDER := half of SYMM2PT minus PARAMETRIX
  168 +define V1COINC := coincidence value [v1] a local scalar of mass dimension
        4
  169 +define JSOURCE := (Box-m^2) applied to REMAINDER
  170 +define JPRIME := primed (Box-m^2) applied to REMAINDER
  171 +define PSOPER := symmetrized point-splitting stress-energy bi-operator
  172 +define RAWTENSOR := coincidence limit of PSOPER applied to REMAINDER
  173 +assume SMOOTHNESS: REMAINDER is smooth on a convex neighborhood of the di
       agonal
  174 +assume HADAMARDRECURSION: parametrix coefficients satisfy the transport e
       quations making (Box-m^2)PARAMETRIX smooth
  175 +assume V0TRANSPORT: exact v0-transport equation killing the N1 pole part
       identically
  176 +assume V1TRANSPORT: exact v1-transport equation making the NL log term C1
        with zero gradient
  177 +assume SYNGE: coincidence derivatives follow the transported-primed-deriv
       ative rule
  178 +assume JPRIMESWAP: primed-source JPRIME satisfies JPRIME(x,x')=JSOURCE(x'
       ,x)
  179 +assume RICCIID: commutator and contracted-Bianchi curvature identities
  180 +assume TAUDATA: first derivatives of the dAlembertian world-function rema
       inder vanish at coincidence
  181 +assume VSYMMWEAK: v1 first-derivative coincidence halves agree
  182 +assume TRANSPORTVANISH: parallel-transport first derivatives vanish at co
       incidence
  183 +claim HOMOGENEOUS: JSOURCE vanishes identically
  184 +derive REMAINDERLIMIT: coincidence limit of JSOURCE equals -3 V1COINC/(4p
       i^2) from [SMOOTHNESS, HADAMARDRECURSION, V0TRANSPORT] | Leibniz on U/sig
       ma and V ln terms with pole and log cancellation plus exact transport
  185 +derive REFUTEHOMOGENEOUS: HOMOGENEOUS is false from [REMAINDERLIMIT] | Mi
       nkowski value -3m^4/(32pi^2) is nonzero for m>0
  186 +derive SPLITTHIRD: primed-half coincidence derivative of JSOURCE equals o
       ne third of the total gradient from [REMAINDERLIMIT, V1TRANSPORT, VSYMMWE
       AK, TAUDATA] | Q1-split 4w over 12w with vanishing log-gradient
  187 +define RESIDUAL := divergence of RAWTENSOR
  188 +derive RESIDUALVALUE: RESIDUAL equals primed-half coincidence derivative
       and equals gradient of -V1COINC/(4pi^2) from [REMAINDERLIMIT, SPLITTHIRD,
        SMOOTHNESS, SYNGE, JPRIMESWAP, RICCIID, TRANSPORTVANISH] | corrected tra
       nsport identity with J/J' distinction plus independent Synge-A route
  189 +define CORRECTION := (1/(4pi^2)) metric times V1COINC
  190 +derive CORRECTIONDIVERGENCE: divergence of CORRECTION equals gradient of
       +V1COINC/(4pi^2) from [RESIDUALVALUE] | metric compatibility with coeffic
       ient forced by cancellation
  191 +derive CORRECTIONNOTCONSERVED: CORRECTION is not independently conserved
       from [CORRECTIONDIVERGENCE] | its nonzero divergence is what cancels the
       residual
  192 +define AMBIGUITY := conserved local geometric tensor of mass dimension 4
  193 +derive AMBIGUITYBASIS: polynomial dimension-four ambiguities spanned by f
       our tensors from [MODEL, FIELD] | metric variation of local scalars is co
       nserved with Lovelock reduction
  194 +define RENTENSOR := RAWTENSOR + CORRECTION + AMBIGUITY
  195 +claim QSGCONSERVATION: divergence of RENTENSOR vanishes
  196 +derive QSGPROOF: QSGCONSERVATION holds from [RESIDUALVALUE, CORRECTIONDIV
       ERGENCE] | opposite gradients cancel and AMBIGUITY is conserved by constr
       uction
  197 +claim NONZEROTRACE: trace of RENTENSOR is generically nonzero
  198 +derive TRACEVALUE: trace equals -(1/2)Box s - m^2 s + V1COINC/(4pi^2) + t
       race AMBIGUITY from [REMAINDERLIMIT, SMOOTHNESS] | operator trace plus co
       rrection trace per N4.8
  199 +claim POLYNOMIALSCOPE: finite ambiguities restricted to polynomial dimens
       ion-four basis
  200 +claim GENERALAMBIGUITY: non-polynomial ambiguities possible beyond renorm
       alizability criterion
  201 +if POLYNOMIALSCOPE:
  202 +    then AMBIGUITYBASIS
  203 +    else GENERALAMBIGUITY
  204 +? Explicit smooth framework-difference term fully identifying the symmetr
       ic route with the Feynman route beyond the derived equation data
  205 +? Full nonlinear line-by-line check of H^(2) conservation beyond the line
       arized check and variation theorem
  206 +```
  207 +
  208 +Conformance self-audit: one construct per line; names plain identifiers
  209 +declared before use, exact-match `from` lists; `define` `:=` throughout;
  210 +every `derive` carries result + premises + reasoning; `if` multiline with
  211 +`then`/`else`; `?` prose-only with what-would-resolve-it (O2-refined,

   79 +func TestGoldenPayloadsStable(t *testing.T) {
   80 +    a, err := qsgt001.Build()
   81 +    if err != nil {
   82 +        t.Fatal(err)
   83 +    }
   84 +    b, err := qsgt001.Build()
   85 +    if err != nil {
   86 +        t.Fatal(err)
   87 +    }
   88 +    gotA := map[string]artifact.ArtifactRef{
   89 +        "combo": a.ComboResult, "cancel": a.CancelResult,
   90 +        "third": a.ThirdResult, "trace": a.TraceResult,
   91 +        "flat": a.FlatResult, "v0": a.V0Shape,
   92 +        "h1": a.H1Shape, "h2": a.H2Shape, "flrw": a.FLRW001,
   93 +    }
   94 +    gotB := map[string]artifact.ArtifactRef{
   95 +        "combo": b.ComboResult, "cancel": b.CancelResult,
   96 +        "third": b.ThirdResult, "trace": b.TraceResult,
   97 +        "flat": b.FlatResult, "v0": b.V0Shape,
   98 +        "h1": b.H1Shape, "h2": b.H2Shape, "flrw": b.FLRW001,
   99 +    }
  100 +    for tag, snapHex := range goldenSnapshots {
  101 +        want, err := hex.DecodeString(snapHex)
  102 +        if err != nil {
  103 +            t.Fatalf("%s: bad snapshot hex: %v", tag, err)
  104 +        }
  105 +        if raw := resolve(t, a.Store, gotA[tag]); !bytes.Equal(raw, want)
        {
  106 +            t.Fatalf("golden %s payload differs from fixed snapshot (regr
       ession break)", tag)
  107 +        }
  108 +        if rawB := resolve(t, b.Store, gotB[tag]); !bytes.Equal(resolve(t
       , a.Store, gotA[tag]), rawB) {
  109 +            t.Fatalf("golden %s payload differs across rebuilds (nondeter
       minism)", tag)
  110 +        }
  111 +    }
  112 +}
  113 +
  114 +func TestAntiGoldenSeparation(t *testing.T) {
  115 +    fx, err := qsgt001.Build()
  116 +    if err != nil {
  117 +        t.Fatal(err)
  118 +    }
  119 +    // Build already gates separation; re-assert from stored refs so the
  120 +    // suite, not just the constructor, owns the falsification regression
       .
  121 +    for tag, pair := range map[string][2]artifact.ArtifactRef{
  122 +        "v0": {fx.V0Shape, fx.V0AntiB},
  123 +        "h1": {fx.H1Shape, fx.H1AntiB},
  124 +        "h2": {fx.H2Shape, fx.H2AntiB},
  125 +    } {
  126 +        if bytes.Equal(resolve(t, fx.Store, pair[0]), resolve(t, fx.Store
       , pair[1])) {
  127 +            t.Fatalf("%s: anti-golden payload equals golden", tag)
  128 +        }
  129 +    }
  130 +}
  131 +
  132 +func TestMutations(t *testing.T) {
  133 +    fx, err := qsgt001.Build()
  134 +    if err != nil {
  135 +        t.Fatal(err)
  136 +    }
  137 +    // Mutant 1: wrong rational input (3+4 instead of 2+4) must not match
  138 +    // the COMBO-6 expected payload.
  139 +    mutantIn, err := physmath.PutExpression(fx.Store, physmath.Add(
  140 +        physmath.MustRational(3, 1), physmath.MustRational(4, 1)))
  141 +    if err != nil {
  142 +        t.Fatal(err)
  143 +    }
  144 +    res, err := physmath.Apply(fx.Store, physmath.Invocation{
  145 +        ProducerNamespace: "physmath",
  146 +        RuleID:            physmath.RuleExprSimplify,
  147 +        InputRefs:         []artifact.ArtifactRef{mutantIn},
  148 +    })
  149 +    if err != nil {
  150 +        t.Fatal(err)
  151 +    }
  152 +    if bytes.Equal(resolve(t, fx.Store, res.OutputRef), resolve(t, fx.Sto
       re, fx.ComboExpected)) {
  153 +        t.Fatal("mutant 3+4 matched the 2+4 golden")
  154 +    }
  155 +    // Mutant 2 (INTEGRITY tripwire, not a replay failure): tampered
  156 +    // derivation bytes must fail Verify. What this proves: Verify's
  157 +    // stored-bytes-vs-re-encode comparison rejects a swapped ref. What
  158 +    // it does NOT prove: replay behavior on a decoded malicious
  159 +    // derivation — there is no codec to decode tampered bytes into a
  160 +    // Derivation struct, so a decoded-malicious replay is untested.
  161 +    _, raw, err := fx.Store.Lookup(fx.DCombo6)
  162 +    if err != nil {
  163 +        t.Fatal(err)
  164 +    }
  165 +    bad := bytes.Clone(raw)
  166 +    bad[len(bad)-1] ^= 0xff
  167 +    badRef, err := fx.Store.Put(physmath.FamilyDerivation, bad)
  168 +    if err != nil {
  169 +        t.Fatal(err)
  170 +    }
  171 +    _ = badRef
  172 +    fx2, err := qsgt001.Build()
  173 +    if err != nil {
  174 +        t.Fatal(err)
  175 +    }
  176 +    fx2.DCombo6 = badRef
  177 +    if err := qsgt001.Verify(fx2); err == nil {
  178 +        t.Fatal("Verify accepted a tampered derivation ref")
  179 +    }
  180 +}
  181 +
  182 +func TestSketchUnsupported(t *testing.T) {
  183 +    fx, err := qsgt001.Build()
  184 +    if err != nil {
  185 +        t.Fatal(err)
  186 +    }
  187 +    if len(fx.Sketch.Steps) == 0 {
  188 +        t.Fatal("empty sketch")
  189 +    }
  190 +    for _, s := range fx.Sketch.Steps {
  191 +        if s.Status != qsgt001.StatusSketchUnsupported {
  192 +            t.Fatalf("sketch %s leaves unsupported status", s.ID)
  193 +        }
  194 +        if s.Evidence == "" {
  195 +            t.Fatalf("sketch %s lacks evidence pointer", s.ID)
  196 +        }
  197 +    }
  198 +    if _, ok := reflect.TypeOf(qsgt001.QSGSketch{}).MethodByName("Verify"
       ); ok {
  199 +        t.Fatal("QSGSketch must not grow a Verify method")
  200 +    }
  201 +    if _, ok := reflect.TypeOf(qsgt001.QSGSketch{}).MethodByName("Replay"
       ); ok {
  202 +        t.Fatal("QSGSketch must not grow a Replay method")
  203 +    }
  204 +}

  206 +type manifestFile struct {
  207 +    SchemaVersion  string   `json:"schema_version"`
  208 +    ArtifactType   string   `json:"artifact_type"`
  209 +    DerivationID   string   `json:"derivation_id"`
  210 +    FrameworkID    string   `json:"framework_id"`
  211 +    Name           string   `json:"name"`
  212 +    EntryPoints    []string `json:"entry_points"`
  213 +    Regime         string   `json:"regime"`
  214 +    Coordinates    []string `json:"coordinates"`
  215 +    Conventions    []string `json:"conventions"`
  216 +    Premises       []string `json:"premises"`
  217 +    Assumptions    []string `json:"assumptions"`
  218 +    ExpectedResult []struct {
  219 +        Statement  string `json:"statement"`
  220 +        ExecutedBy string `json:"executed_by"`
  221 +    } `json:"expected_result"`
  222 +    Verification       []string `json:"verification"`
  223 +    Limitations        []string `json:"limitations"`
  224 +    Anomalies          []string `json:"anomalies"`
  225 +    FalsificationConds []string `json:"falsification_conditions"`
  226 +}
  227 +
  228 +func TestManifestStrict(t *testing.T) {
  229 +    raw := areaFile(t, "manifest.json")
  230 +    var mf manifestFile
  231 +    dec := json.NewDecoder(bytes.NewReader(raw))
  232 +    dec.DisallowUnknownFields()
  233 +    if err := dec.Decode(&mf); err != nil {
  234 +        t.Fatalf("manifest strict decode: %v", err)
  235 +    }
  236 +    // Unknown-field injection: strict decoding must REJECT a manifest
  237 +    // carrying a field the struct does not declare.
  238 +    var m map[string]any
  239 +    if err := json.Unmarshal(raw, &m); err != nil {
  240 +        t.Fatal(err)
  241 +    }
  242 +    m["unknown_probe_field"] = 1
  243 +    bad, err := json.Marshal(m)
  244 +    if err != nil {
  245 +        t.Fatal(err)
  246 +    }
  247 +    var mf2 manifestFile
  248 +    dec2 := json.NewDecoder(bytes.NewReader(bad))
  249 +    dec2.DisallowUnknownFields()
  250 +    if err := dec2.Decode(&mf2); err == nil {
  251 +        t.Fatal("manifest strict decode accepted an unknown field")
  252 +    }
  253 +    if len(mf.ExpectedResult) == 0 || len(mf.Limitations) < 3 || len(mf.A
       ssumptions) == 0 {
  254 +        t.Fatal("manifest missing expected_result/limitations/assumptions
       ")
  255 +    }
  256 +    joinedExp := ""
  257 +    for _, e := range mf.ExpectedResult {
  258 +        if e.ExecutedBy == "" {
  259 +            t.Fatalf("expected_result entry without executed_by: %q", e.S
       tatement)
  260 +        }
  261 +        joinedExp += e.Statement + "\n"
  262 +    }
  263 +    if strings.Contains(joinedExp, "universal") {
  264 +        t.Fatal("expected_result must not claim the universal identity")
  265 +    }
  266 +    joinedLim := strings.Join(mf.Limitations, "\n")
  267 +    if !strings.Contains(joinedLim, "universal") {
  268 +        t.Fatal("limitations must state the universal identity is unexecu
       ted")
  269 +    }
  270 +    joinedAnom := strings.Join(mf.Anomalies, "\n")
  271 +    if !strings.Contains(joinedAnom, "E1") || !strings.Contains(joinedAno
       m, "E2") {
  272 +        t.Fatal("anomalies must record errata E1 and E2")
  273 +    }
  274 +    if !strings.Contains(joinedAnom, "E3") {
  275 +        t.Fatal("anomalies must record repair errata E3/E4/E5")
  276 +    }
  277 +}

  279 +type sidecarFile struct {
  280 +    ProvenanceNote string `json:"provenance_note"`
  281 +    Claims         []struct {
  282 +        ID            string   `json:"id"`
  283 +        Title         string   `json:"title"`
  284 +        Status        string   `json:"status"`
  285 +        MathStatus    string   `json:"math_status"`
  286 +        MachineStatus string   `json:"machine_status"`
  287 +        EvidenceRefs  []string `json:"evidence_refs"`
  288 +    } `json:"claims"`
  289 +    Obligations []struct {
  290 +        ID               string   `json:"id"`
  291 +        Statement        string   `json:"statement"`
  292 +        Blocks           []string `json:"blocks"`
  293 +        EvidenceRequired string   `json:"evidence_required"`
  294 +    } `json:"obligations"`
  295 +}

  297 +func TestSidecar(t *testing.T) {
  298 +    raw := areaFile(t, "research_metadata.json")
  299 +    var sc sidecarFile
  300 +    dec := json.NewDecoder(bytes.NewReader(raw))
  301 +    dec.DisallowUnknownFields()
  302 +    if err := dec.Decode(&sc); err != nil {
  303 +        t.Fatalf("sidecar strict decode: %v", err)
  304 +    }
  305 +    // Unknown-field injection: strict decoding must REJECT a sidecar
  306 +    // carrying a field the struct does not declare.
  307 +    var m map[string]any
  308 +    if err := json.Unmarshal(raw, &m); err != nil {
  309 +        t.Fatal(err)
  310 +    }
  311 +    m["unknown_probe_field"] = 1
  312 +    bad, err := json.Marshal(m)
  313 +    if err != nil {
  314 +        t.Fatal(err)
  315 +    }
  316 +    var sc2 sidecarFile
  317 +    dec2 := json.NewDecoder(bytes.NewReader(bad))
  318 +    dec2.DisallowUnknownFields()
  319 +    if err := dec2.Decode(&sc2); err == nil {
  320 +        t.Fatal("sidecar strict decode accepted an unknown field")
  321 +    }
  322 +    seenTheorem := false
  323 +    for _, c := range sc.Claims {
  324 +        if strings.Contains(c.Status, "machine-proved") || strings.Contai
       ns(c.Status, "machine-verified") {
  325 +            t.Fatalf("claim %s asserts machine proof: %q", c.ID, c.Status
       )
  326 +        }
  327 +        if c.ID == "QSG-T001" {
  328 +            seenTheorem = true
  329 +            if !strings.Contains(c.Status, "machine-unexecuted") {
  330 +                t.Fatalf("QSG-T001 must be machine-unexecuted, got %q", c
       .Status)
  331 +            }
  332 +        }
  333 +    }
  334 +    if !seenTheorem {
  335 +        t.Fatal("QSG-T001 claim missing")
  336 +    }
  337 +    wantO := map[string]bool{"O1": false, "O2": false, "O3": false, "O4":
        false, "O5": false}
  338 +    for _, o := range sc.Obligations {
  339 +        if _, ok := wantO[o.ID]; !ok {
  340 +            t.Fatalf("unexpected obligation %s", o.ID)
  341 +        }
  342 +        wantO[o.ID] = true
  343 +        if o.EvidenceRequired == "" {
  344 +            t.Fatalf("obligation %s lacks evidence_required", o.ID)
  345 +        }
  346 +    }
  347 +    for id, seen := range wantO {
  348 +        if !seen {
  349 +            t.Fatalf("obligation %s missing", id)
  350 +        }
  351 +    }
  352 +}

  correction +g[v₁]/(4π²), cancellation exact, A-data (0,0). No DF number is a

◆ This is a plan, not a special mode; I haven't started implementation. Reply go

  Goal

  Implement plan7.md: establish Bohmian equivariance as Benchmark 2 (second
  physics vertical slice), reusing the shared platform without polluting
  PhysMath, then evaluate what the two benchmarks jointly prove about
  cross-theory generality. No new QSG-T001 review cycle.

  Success Criteria

  • A bounded equivariance derivation exists from stated premises (wavefunction
    → density/current → continuity → guidance → flow → equivariance), with an
    assumptions register, local-vs-global separation, Math Reality Check
    regime/scope, and per-step dispositions (derived / source-reported /
    conditional / unsupported).
  • An executable artifact exists following minisuperspace + qsg-t001
    conventions (manifest, protocol tests, honest supported-subset tests, no
    conclusion-imported goldens), with Bohmian commitments inside phys-lib/bohm
    and PhysMath theory-neutral.
  • The five platform-evaluation questions are answered with evidence, plus a
    QSG-vs-Bohm representation comparison and a prioritized demonstrated-gap
    list. No preemptive refactoring.
  • Benchmark 1's status inconsistency from plan6_imp_review.md §4 is resolved
    as bookkeeping (O1 verification-level split + provisional wording), with
    zero QSG re-derivation.
  • Shared-repo diff audited and left uncommitted; frozen repos (phys, phys-gr)
    untouched.

  Approach

  Three discoveries shape this plan. First, phys-lib/bohm/doc.go already owns
  exactly this remit ("Bohmian-mechanics framework semantics… mathematical
  execution stays in phys-math"), and phys-lib/bohm/minisuperspace/ is a prior
  in-repo artifact (fixture, manifest, protocol, tests) — so placement and
  conventions are precedented, not designed fresh. Second, plan7.md (01:50) is
  newer than plan6_imp_review.md (01:46) and explicitly forbids another QSG
  review cycle — so the review is dispositioned as bounded status bookkeeping,
  and its narrow-audit evidence becomes a packaged reading list for future
  external replication, not new work. Third, unlike QSG (TOE-v6 only), this
  mission writes to the shared repo — so the plan carries a git-diff audit and
  no-commit boundary throughout.

  Steps

  1. Baseline + status alignment (bookkeeping only, no re-derivation). Run the
     QSG v2 gate as a health check; record the O1 four-level split (in-execution
     derivation / internal cross-check closed; external replication /
     separate-session reproduction open) and the provisional-acceptance wording
     in the QSG sidecar/manifest/06-O1; record the J/J′ non-exchange rule as a
     proposed permanent methodology note; package the narrow-audit reading list
     (08 N2–N4, doc 04 J/J′, doc 06, v2 tests).
  2. Read-first recon. phys-system/AGENTS.md, phys-math/AGENTS.md, phys-lib
     README + PROTOCOL_GAPS, bohm/doc.go + full minisuperspace (check for
     existing continuity/flow/equivariance coverage to avoid duplication); map
     reusable PhysMath abstractions vs genuine gaps; confirm placement
     (recommend: Go in phys-lib/bohm/equivariance/ per minisuperspace precedent,
     math record in new research/experiments/bohm-preflight-001/ mirroring
     qsg-preflight-001); record shared-repo git status baseline.
  3. Math derivation. Nonrelativistic finite-particle Schrödinger chain per
     mission scope; assumptions register (regularity, node singularities of
     v=j/ρ, boundary/decay, flow existence); local-in-time first, global only
     under explicit existence premises (expect CONDITIONAL/unsupported — honest,
     not forced); MRC regime/scope statement reusing the BHC MRC convention;
     dispositions per result. Stop conditions enforced: no QFT, relativistic,
     measurement, or interpretation-comparison content (BHC R3 typicality/Born
     routes explicitly out).
  4. Implementation. Theory commitments in phys-lib/bohm; no new PhysMath
     abstractions unless the math requires (deficiency-driven only); manifest +
     protocol/derivation_test.go + sketch pattern mirroring qsg-t001 v2; tests
     verify only the supported subset; derivation conclusion never a golden
     input.
  5. Platform evaluation + handoff. Answer the five mission questions with
     file-level evidence; write the QSG-vs-Bohm comparison and prioritized gap
     list; full gates (build/test/vet/gofmt both scopes, JSON validity);
     shared-repo diff audit, everything left uncommitted; TOE-v6 hash manifest;
     report.

  Validation Plan

  • QSG v2 gate green before starting (baseline health): go build/test/vet,
    gofmt -l empty in theorems/qsg-t001/ with the colocated
    GOWORK/GOCACHE/GOPROXY=off recipe.
  • New package + artifact: go build ./..., go test -count=1 ./...,
    go vet ./..., gofmt -l empty; manifest/sidecar strict-decode +
    unknown-field-injection tests; snapshot/determinism tests; sketch exposes no
    Verify/Replay (mirror v2 suite properties).
  • Highest-risk check (manual, non-automatable): audit that every proof
    obligation is either derived-from-premises or explicitly marked
    conditional/unsupported, and that no test or golden smuggles the conclusion
    — the same honesty audit that caught the QSG defects.
  • Boundary checks: git status/diff in /home/chaschel/Documents/go shows only
    intended phys-lib/bohm additions; frozen phys, phys-gr, committed root
    go.work untouched; nothing committed.

  Risks / Open Questions

  • Shared-repo writes are new for this programme (QSG wrote only TOE-v6):
    mitigated by baseline + final diff audit and the no-commit rule.
  • Global Bohmian flow existence is genuinely hard (nodes, measure-theoretic
    premises): treated as expected-CONDITIONAL scope, not a failure; local
    result is the deliverable floor.
  • No open questions blocking planning: artifact naming (bohm-preflight-001)
    and package layout are reversible execution details; the mission is
    otherwise prescriptive.

❯ Plan 7.1 is the right next move. Approved for execution, with two small
  safeguards. I would not create another planning or review cycle.

  It follows the strategic direction we agreed on: use Bohmian equivariance as
  the second physics vertical slice, reuse the existing platform, identify
  actual mathematical gaps, and avoid preemptive architectural work.&#x20;

  plan7.1.md



  ### Two safeguards before execution

  1\. Make the theorem's mathematical scope explicit.

  The plan should freeze the basic system being formalized: for example, a
  finite collection of spinless particles governed by the standard
  nonrelativistic Schrödinger equation, with clearly stated masses and
  potential.

  Then require the central result to be expressed as preservation of the
  probability distribution under the Bohmian configuration-space flow:

  \\[ (\Phi\_{t,t_0})\_\\#\rho\_{t_0}=\rho_t. \\]

  This distinguishes the continuity equation from the actual equivariance
  result. The latter requires sufficient conditions for the flow and its
  probability distribution to exist. Keep local and global conclusions separate,
  as the plan already proposes.

  2\. Do not overstate what two benchmarks establish.

  QSG-T001 and Bohmian equivariance will give us evidence that common
  mathematical representations can support two substantially different physical
  frameworks.

  They will not prove that PhysMath is universally adequate for physics. Call
  the result an initial cross-theory coverage assessment. That's a meaningful
  milestone without overstating it.

  ### Execution priorities

  I would preserve the rest of the plan, with one operational rule:

  - QSG bookkeeping is bounded maintenance, not another research project.
  - Inspect existing Bohmian code before implementing anything.
  - Derive first, implement second.
  - Add abstractions only when an actual mathematical requirement exposes a
  deficiency.
  - Keep the shared repository uncommitted and audit the final diff.
  - Do not expand into relativistic Bohmian mechanics, QFT, typicality, or
  measurement theory.

  ### What success should give us

  The most valuable deliverable is not another theorem by itself. It is the
  comparison between the two:

  - QSG-T001: curved-spacetime geometry, bitensors, local subtraction and
  conservation.
  - Bohmian equivariance: probability density, current, continuity equations and
  dynamical flow.

  From that comparison, we learn what mathematics genuinely belongs in PhysMath,
  what belongs in theory-specific libraries, and what our current platform
  cannot yet express.

  My decision: proceed with plan7.1. No more QSG reviews. The next strategic
  milestone is the completed Bohmian artifact and the evidence-based
  mathematical coverage assessment.

    equivariance; local/global separate.
  • S2 (wording): Step 5 deliverable is titled an initial cross-theory coverage
    assessment; universal-adequacy language banned.

◆ Wrote research/experiments/bohm-preflight-001/audit/01-premises-assumptions.md
 (+121)
    1 +# 01 — Premises, system freeze, assumptions register
    2 +
    3 +Programme: Bohmian equivariance, Benchmark 2 (plan7 + plan7.1, approval
    4 +safeguards S1/S2). Author: executor. Date: 2026-11-06.
    5 +
    6 +Dispositions follow the QSG convention: DERIVED (shown here from stated
    7 +premises), SOURCE-REPORTED (standard result cited with inspected source),
    8 +CONDITIONAL (holds under an explicit undischarged premise), UNSUPPORTED
    9 +(not established here), INDEPENDENTLY-REPRODUCED ( Blind re-derivation
   10 +agrees — not claimed unless performed).
   11 +
   12 +---
   13 +
   14 +## 1. System freeze (S1 — binding on docs 02–03 and the fixture)
   15 +
   16 +- Finite collection of \(N \ge 1\) spinless, distinguishable particles in
   17 +  three-dimensional Euclidean space. Configuration space
   18 +  \(\mathcal Q = \mathbb R^{3N}\), points \(q=(q_1,\dots,q_N)\).
   19 +- Masses \(m_1,\dots,m_N > 0\) (stated, distinct symbols; no
   20 +  equal-mass assumption).
   21 +- Real-valued potential \(V:\mathcal Q\to\mathbb R\) (regularity per B3).
   22 +- Wavefunction \(\psi:\mathbb R\times\mathcal Q\to\mathbb C\) governed by
   23 +  the standard nonrelativistic Schrödinger equation
   24 +  \[ i\hbar\,\partial_t\psi = H\psi,\qquad
   25 +     H = \sum_{k=1}^N T_k + V,\qquad
   26 +     T_k = -\frac{\hbar^2}{2m_k}\Delta_k, \]
   27 +  with \(\Delta_k\) the Laplacian in \(q_k\). \(\hbar\) and \(m_k\) stay
   28 +  explicit (no \(\hbar=1\)); the Bohmian current carries \(\hbar/m_k\).
   29 +- Density \(\rho = |\psi|^2 = \psi^*\psi\).
   30 +- Current (per particle) \(j_k = \frac{\hbar}{m_k}\,
   31 +  \mathrm{Im}(\psi^*\nabla_k\psi)\), \(\nabla_k\) the gradient in \(q_k\)
       .
   32 +- Guidance velocity \(v_k = j_k/\rho\) on the open set \(\{\rho>0\}\);
   33 +  undefined at nodes \(\{\rho=0\}\) (B5).
   34 +- Flow \(\Phi_{t,t_0}\) of \(\dot q_k = v_k(t,q)\) where it exists (B6/B7
       ).
   35 +
   36 +## 2. Central result (frozen statement; proof in doc 03)
   37 +
   38 +Let \(\rho_t(q) = |\psi_t(q)|^2\). Local form: on any time interval
   39 +where the Bohmian flow exists and \(\rho_t\) is sufficiently regular,
   40 +\[ (\Phi_{t,t_0})_\#\,\rho_{t_0} = \rho_t, \]
   41 +i.e. the pushforward of the initial distribution under the
   42 +configuration-space flow equals the Schrödinger-evolved distribution.
   43 +Global form: the same identity for all \(t\in\mathbb R\) under global
   44 +flow-existence premises. The continuity equation
   45 +\(\partial_t\rho + \sum_k\nabla_k\!\cdot\! j_k = 0\) is a LEMMA, not the
   46 +result: equivariance additionally requires the flow, the transport
   47 +identity, and uniqueness for the continuity equation. Local and global
   48 +conclusions are established (or not) separately.
   49 +
   50 +## 3. Assumptions register
   51 +
   52 +- B1 (dynamics): \(\psi\) satisfies the frozen Schrödinger equation
   53 +  strongly enough that \(\partial_t\rho\) exists pointwise
   54 +  (\(\psi,\partial_t\psi\) continuous). Premise.
   55 +- B2 (regularity): \(\nabla_k\psi\), \(\Delta_k\psi\) continuous on
   56 +  \(\mathbb R\times\mathcal Q\) (so \(\rho, j_k\) are \(C^1\) and the
   57 +  continuity computation is classical). Premise; minimal sufficient
   58 +  regularity recorded in doc 02 if weaker conditions suffice.
   59 +- B3 (potential): \(V\) real-valued; sufficient regularity for B1–B2 to
   60 +  hold (smooth bounded \(V\) certainly suffices; singular/Coulomb-type
   61 +  \(V\) NOT included unless doc 02 derives the needed domain facts —
   62 +  default excluded).
   63 +- B4 (decay/boundary): \(\psi,\nabla_k\psi\to 0\) at infinity fast
   64 +  enough that boundary flux vanishes when integrated conservation is
   65 +  asserted. Needed ONLY for global/integrated statements, never for
   66 +  the local continuity identity. Premise where used, flagged at use.
   67 +- B5 (nodes): \(v_k\) is defined only on \(\{\rho>0\}\); no assertion
   68 +  is made about trajectories hitting, leaving, or crossing the nodal
   69 +  set \(\{\rho=0\}\) unless an explicit node premise is stated and
   70 +  sourced. Standing restriction.
   71 +- B6 (local flow): on \(\{\rho>0\}\) with B2, \(v\) is locally
   72 +  Lipschitz (quotient of \(C^1\) functions over nonvanishing
   73 +  denominator), hence the ODE \(\dot q=v(t,q)\) has unique local
   74 +  solutions (standard ODE theory, SOURCE-REPORTED at use).
   75 +- B7 (global flow + transport): existence of \(\Phi_{t,t_0}\) for all
   76 +  time, non-arrival at nodes in finite time, and uniqueness for the
   77 +  continuity equation. NOT derived from B1–B6 here; either imported
   78 +  with an inspected primary source (Dürr–Goldstein–Zanghì-type
   79 +  results) or the global conclusion stays CONDITIONAL/unsupported.
   80 +  Decision recorded in doc 03 with evidence.
   81 +
   82 +## 4. Exclusions (stop conditions, binding)
   83 +
   84 +Spin; relativistic Bohmian mechanics; quantum field theory;
   85 +measurement theory, collapse, or Born-rule justification
   86 +(typicality/BHC-R3 routes out); interpretation comparison;
   87 +backreaction/self-gravity; singular potentials (default, per B3).
   88 +
   89 +## 5. Recon decisions carried in (Step 1 record)
   90 +
   91 +- Placement: Go artifact in `phys-lib/bohm/equivariance/` (mission
   92 +  constraint + minisuperspace precedent); math record here
   93 +  (`bohm-preflight-001/audit/`, single-executor layout rather than
   94 +  the QSG multi-agent tree).
   95 +- Reuse map: physmath expression/function/relation/condition/
   96 +  derivation/equation/definition families; rules CANON, SIMPLIFY,
   97 +  DIFFERENTIATE-001 (+ TOTAL-DERIVATIVE/SUBSTITUTE/RAT-NORMALIZE
   98 +  only if the derivation consumes them); Encode/Validate/Replay;
   99 +  NEQ conditions; minisuperspace household (Build/Verify, Derivs
  100 +  replay, anti-goldens incl. historical-wrong-path pattern,
  101 +  limitation-record + coverage-audit tests, source_mapping sidecar
  102 +  + structural test). Expected gaps (verify at implementation): no
  103 +  integration rule, no ODE/flow execution, no measure/pushforward
  104 +  execution, PDE existence unrepresentable → sketch/limitations.
  105 +- Manifest: GR 16-key shape mirrored from minisuperspace; gaps
  106 +  registered (expected_result + falsification absent; source
  107 +  relocated to sidecar per maxwellbox precedent — frozen
  108 +  phys-trace reads ONLY the minisuperspace manifest path, so no
  109 +  in-place consumer constrains us); NO research_metadata.json in
  110 +  repo (minisuperspace precedent; epistemic tracking lives here).
  111 +- gr/equivariance (algebraic \(f(\rho_X)=\rho_Y(f)\)) is the §3.3
  112 +  comparison foil for doc 04, not a premise.
  113 +- No subagent cross-check: mission neither requires nor forbids;
  114 +  the chain is standard textbook material whose risks are
  115 +  assumption-explicitness (handled by B-register + dispositions),
  116 +  not coincidence calculus; recorded limitation.
  117 +- phys-math untouched expected (deficiency-driven only); shared
  118 +  go commands need writable GOCACHE (sandbox default read-only);
  119 +  phys-lib git baseline clean except pre-existing untracked
  120 +  `.kilo/`; frozen `phys`/`phys-gr` + root go.work never touched;
  121 +  nothing committed.

◆ Wrote research/experiments/bohm-preflight-001/audit/02-continuity-derivation.m
d (+111)
    1 +# 02 — Continuity equation (DERIVED lemma)
    2 +
    3 +Parent: doc 01 (S1 freeze, B-register). This document derives the
    4 +continuity equation from the frozen Schrödinger dynamics. It is a
    5 +LEMMA for equivariance, not equivariance itself: no flow, no
    6 +transport, no pushforward appears here.
    7 +
    8 +---
    9 +
   10 +## 1. Current-definition identities (DERIVED)
   11 +
   12 +From S1: \(j_k = (\hbar/m_k)\,\mathrm{Im}(\psi^*\nabla_k\psi)\).
   13 +(i) \(j_k\) is real-valued (imaginary part of a complex field).
   14 +(ii) Equivalent form:
   15 +\[ j_k = \frac{\hbar}{2im_k}
   16 +       (\psi^*\nabla_k\psi - \psi\nabla_k\psi^*), \]
   17 +since \(z - z^* = 2i\,\mathrm{Im}\,z\) with \(z=\psi^*\nabla_k\psi\).
   18 +Both forms are used below; they are algebraically identical, no
   19 +approximation. Consumes: definitions only.
   20 +
   21 +## 2. Continuity derivation (DERIVED)
   22 +
   23 +\(\rho = \psi^*\psi\). By B1–B2 all derivatives below exist
   24 +classically and the product/chain rules apply pointwise.
   25 +
   26 +Step 1 — time derivative:
   27 +\[ \partial_t\rho
   28 +   = (\partial_t\psi^*)\psi + \psi^*(\partial_t\psi). \]
   29 +
   30 +Step 2 — Schrödinger equation and its conjugate. From S1,
   31 +\(i\hbar\,\partial_t\psi = H\psi\), i.e.
   32 +\(\partial_t\psi = -(i/\hbar)H\psi\).
   33 +\(H\) has real coefficients (\(T_k\) real differential operator,
   34 +\(V\) real by B3), so conjugating gives
   35 +\(-i\hbar\,\partial_t\psi^* = (H\psi)^*\), i.e.
   36 +\(\partial_t\psi^* = +(i/\hbar)(H\psi)^*\).
   37 +The relative sign is the load-bearing step; it is checked twice
   38 +in §4.
   39 +
   40 +Step 3 — potential cancellation. Substituting:
   41 +\[ \partial_t\rho
   42 +   = \frac{i}{\hbar}\big[(H\psi)^*\psi - \psi^*H\psi\big]. \]
   43 +Write \(H = T + V\) (\(T=\sum_k T_k\)). The potential pieces:
   44 +\((V\psi)^*\psi - \psi^*V\psi = V|\psi|^2 - V|\psi|^2 = 0\)
   45 +using \(V\) real (B3). Only kinetic terms survive:
   46 +\[ \partial_t\rho
   47 +   = \frac{i}{\hbar}\sum_k\big[(T_k\psi)^*\psi - \psi^*T_k\psi\big].
   48 +\]
   49 +
   50 +Step 4 — kinetic term as divergence. Per particle,
   51 +\((T_k\psi)^*\psi - \psi^*T_k\psi
   52 += -(\hbar^2/2m_k)[(\Delta_k\psi)^*\psi - \psi^*\Delta_k\psi]\).
   53 +Divergence identity (product rule, B2):
   54 +\[ \nabla_k\!\cdot\!(\psi^*\nabla_k\psi - \psi\nabla_k\psi^*)
   55 +   = \psi^*\Delta_k\psi - \psi\Delta_k\psi^*, \]
   56 +because the cross terms \((\nabla_k\psi^*)\!\cdot\!(\nabla_k\psi)\)
   57 +cancel. Hence
   58 +\((\Delta_k\psi)^*\psi - \psi^*\Delta_k\psi
   59 += -\nabla_k\!\cdot\!(\psi^*\nabla_k\psi - \psi\nabla_k\psi^*)\).
   60 +With \(\psi^*\nabla_k\psi - \psi\nabla_k\psi^*
   61 += 2i\,\mathrm{Im}(\psi^*\nabla_k\psi)\):
   62 +\[ (T_k\psi)^*\psi - \psi^*T_k\psi
   63 +   = -\frac{\hbar^2}{2m_k}\cdot\big[-2i\,
   64 +      \nabla_k\!\cdot\!\mathrm{Im}(\psi^*\nabla_k\psi)\big]
   65 +   = \frac{i\hbar^2}{m_k}\,
   66 +      \nabla_k\!\cdot\!\mathrm{Im}(\psi^*\nabla_k\psi). \]
   67 +
   68 +Step 5 — assembly:
   69 +\[ \partial_t\rho
   70 +   = \frac{i}{\hbar}\sum_k \frac{i\hbar^2}{m_k}\,
   71 +      \nabla_k\!\cdot\!\mathrm{Im}(\psi^*\nabla_k\psi)
   72 +   = -\sum_k \nabla_k\!\cdot\!
   73 +      \left[\frac{\hbar}{m_k}\mathrm{Im}(\psi^*\nabla_k\psi)\right], \]
   74 +using \(i\cdot i = -1\) and \(\hbar^2/\hbar = \hbar\). That is
   75 +\[ \boxed{
   76 +   \partial_t\rho + \sum_{k=1}^N \nabla_k\!\cdot\! j_k = 0.
   77 +   }
   78 +\tag{C}
   79 +\]
   80 +Disposition: DERIVED from S1+B1+B2+B3. Consumed exactly: B1
   81 +(\(\partial_t\) exists), B2 (classical product rule + \(\Delta_k\)),
   82 +B3 (\(V\) real, Step 3). NOT consumed: B4 (no integration), B5–B7
   83 +(no flow, no nodes, no transport).
   84 +
   85 +## 3. What (C) does and does not establish
   86 +
   87 +ESTABLISHED: pointwise differential identity for every classical
   88 +solution; \(j_k\) real; \(V\)-independence of the current (any real
   89 +\(V\) gives the same \((C)\) — the potential affects \(\psi\), not
   90 +the identity's form). NOT established: conservation of total
   91 +probability (needs B4 integration — stated, not derived, until
   92 +doc 03 needs it); anything about trajectories, flow, nodes, or
   93 +equivariance; anything about non-solutions (for arbitrary test
   94 +\(\psi\), (C) need not hold — this becomes a fixture NEGATIVE
   95 +check, doc 03 §5).
   96 +
   97 +## 4. Self-checks (both performed)
   98 +
   99 +(i) Sign audit: \(\partial_t\psi\) carries \(-i/\hbar\);
  100 +\(\partial_t\psi^*\) carries \(+i/\hbar\); Step 5 gives
  101 +\(i\cdot i = -1\), matching the \(+\sum\nabla\!\cdot\! j\) on the
  102 +left of (C). Recomputed independently from (ii)-form of \(j_k\):
  103 +identical result.
  104 +(ii) Free-particle plane wave \(\psi = e^{i(p\cdot q - Et)/\hbar}\):
  105 +\(\rho = 1\), \(j_k = p_k/m_k\) constant, \(\partial_t\rho = 0\),
  106 +\(\nabla_k\!\cdot\! j_k = 0\): (C) holds \(0=0\). (Plane wave is
  107 +not \(L^2\); used only as an algebraic check of coefficients,
  108 +never as a physical state.)
  109 +(iii) Dimensional audit: \([\rho]=L^{-3N}\),
  110 +\([j_k]=[\hbar/m]\cdot L^{-3N-1} = (L^2T^{-1})(L^{-3N-1})\),
  111 +\([\nabla_k\!\cdot\! j_k]=L^{-3N}T^{-1}=[\partial_t\rho]\). ✓

├ \[quant-ph/0406094\] A Relativistic Version of theGhirardi–Rimini–Weber Model
│
│ # A Relativistic Version of the
│ Ghirardi–Rimini–Weber Model
│
│  Roderich Tumulka Note: Dipartimento di Fisica dell’Università di Genova and I
│ NFN sezione di Genova, Via Dodecaneso 33, 16146 Genova, Italy. E-mail: tumulka
│ @mathematik.uni-muenchen.de
│
│ August 16, 2006
│
│ ###### Abstract
│
│ Carrying out a research program outlined by John S. Bell in 1987, we arrive at
│  a relativistic version of the Ghirardi–Rimini–Weber (GRW) model of spontaneou
│ s wavefunction collapse. The GRW model was proposed as a solution of the measu
│ rement problem of quantum mechanics and involves a stochastic and nonlinear mo
│ dification of the Schrödinger equation. It deviates very little from the Schrö
│ dinger equation for microscopic systems but efficiently suppresses, for macros
│ copic systems, superpositions of macroscopically different states. As suggeste
│ d by Bell, we take the primitive ontology, or local beables, of our model to b
│ e a discrete set of space-time points, at which the collapses are centered. Th
│ is set is random with distribution determined by the initial wavefunction. Our
│  model is nonlocal and violates Bell’s inequality though it does not make use
│ of a preferred slicing of space-time or any other sort of synchronization of s
│ pacelike separated points. Like the GRW model, it reproduces the quantum proba
│ bilities in all cases presently testable, though it entails deviations from th
│ e quantum formalism that are in principle testable. Our model works in Minkows
│ ki space-time as well as in (well-behaved) curved background space-times.
│
│ PACS numbers: 03.65.Ta; 03.65.Ud; 03.30.+p. Key words: spontaneous wavefunctio
│ n collapse; relativity; quantum theory without observers.
│
│ ## 1 Introduction
│
│ > …I am particularly struck by the fact that the \[GRW\] model is as Lorentz i
│ nvariant as it could be in the nonrelativistic version. It takes away the grou
│ nd of my fear that any exact formulation of quantum mechanics must conflict wi
│ th fundamental Lorentz invariance. J. S. Bell \[[4](#bib.bib4 "")\]
│
│ In 1986, Ghirardi, Rimini, and Weber (GRW) proposed a model of spontaneous wav
│ efunction collapse \[[19](#bib.bib19 "")\] based on a stochastic and nonlinear
│  modification of the Schrödinger equation. When combined with a clear ontology
│ , the GRW model turns quantum mechanics into a completely coherent theory. It
│ resolves all paradoxes of quantum mechanics, in particular the measurement pro
│ blem, and accounts for all phenomena of quantum mechanics in terms of an objec
│ tive reality governed by mathematical laws. However, the GRW model is nonrelat
│ ivistic.
│
│ In \[[4](#bib.bib4 "")\] and again in \[[6](#bib.bib6 "")\], John S. Bell emph
│ asized that the GRW model has a property—multi-time translation invariance—tha
│ t can be regarded as a nonrelativistic surrogate of Lorentz invariance. This f
│ act suggests that the biggest difficulty one would expect with turning a nonre
│ lativistic theory into a relativistic one—the difficulty caused by the lack of
│  a temporal ordering of spacelike separated events—is absent in the GRW model
│ right from the start. We find this suggestion to be correct, and can indeed sp
│ ecify a relativistic version of the GRW model. We proceed along the lines of B
│ ell’s suggestions; in particular, we do not use a *continuous* spontaneous col
│ lapse model (corresponding to a diffusion process in Hilbert space and known a
│ s continuous spontaneous localization, or CSL), but rather a *discrete* one co
│ rresponding to a jump process in Hilbert space. Furthermore, we follow Bell in
│  taking as the primitive ontology, or local beables, of the model the space-ti
│ me points where the collapses are centered \[[4](#bib.bib4 ""), [6](#bib.bib6
│ ""), [21](#bib.bib21 "")\]. “A piece of matter then is a galaxy of such events
│ ” \[[4](#bib.bib4 "")\]. We will call these points “flashes.”
│
│ For a recent overview of spontaneous collapse models, see \[[3](#bib.bib3 "")\
│ ]. We regard our model as a step towards one possible explanation of the proba
│ bility rules of quantum theory in the relativistic realm. This realm differs i
│ n two w
│
│ [Answer truncated]

├ \[math-ph/0406030\] Simple Proof for Global Existence of Bohmian Trajectories
│
│ # Simple Proof for Global Existence of Bohmian Trajectories
│
│  Stefan Teufel Note: Mathematics Institute, University of Warwick, CV4 7AL Cov
│ entry, UK. E-mail: teufel@maths.warwick.ac.uk    Roderich Tumulka Note: Dipart
│ imento di Fisica dell’Università di Genova and INFN sezione di Genova, Via Dod
│ ecaneso 33, 16146 Genova, Italy. E-mail: tumulka@mathematik.uni-muenchen.de
│
│ June 15, 2004
│
│ ###### Abstract
│
│ We address the question whether Bohmian trajectories exist for all times. Bohm
│ ian trajectories are solutions of an ordinary differential equation involving
│ a wavefunction obeying either the Schrödinger or the Dirac equation. Some traj
│ ectories may end in finite time, for example by running into a node of the wav
│ efunction, where the law of motion is ill-defined. The aim is to show, under s
│ uitable assumptions on the initial wavefunction and the potential, global exis
│ tence of almost all solutions. We provide a simpler and more transparent proof
│  of the known global existence result for spinless Schrödinger particles and e
│ xtend the result to particles with spin, to the presence of magnetic fields, a
│ nd to Dirac wavefunctions. Our main new result are conditions on the current v
│ ector field on configuration-space-time which are sufficient for almost-sure g
│ lobal existence.
│
│ MSC (2000): 34A12; 81P99. PACS: 02.30.Hq; 03.65.Ta; 03.65.Pm. Key words: Bohmi
│ an mechanics; ordinary differential equations: existence of solutions; equivar
│ iant probability distribution; current vector field; Schrödinger equation; Dir
│ ac equation.
│
│ ## 1 Introduction
│
│ We study a mathematical question arising from and relevant to Bohmian mechanic
│ s \[[5](#bib.bib5 ""), [1](#bib.bib1 ""), [11](#bib.bib11 ""), [3](#bib.bib3 "
│ "), [10](#bib.bib10 "")\] and its variant based on the Dirac equation \[[6](#b
│ ib.bib6 ""), [7](#bib.bib7 "")\] (henceforth referred to as the “Bohm–Dirac th
│ eory”). In these theories, the motion of particles is defined by ordinary diff
│ erential equations (ODEs) involving the wavefunction, see ([3](#S2.E3 "In 2.1
│ Equations of Motion ‣ 2 Setup ‣ Simple Proof for Global Existence of Bohmian T
│ rajectories")) and ([5](#S2.E5 "In 2.1 Equations of Motion ‣ 2 Setup ‣ Simple
│ Proof for Global Existence of Bohmian Trajectories")) below. The mathematical
│ question we address is global existence, i.e., whether (under what conditions
│ and how often) the particle trajectories are well defined for all times. One o
│ bstruction to global existence is that the velocity given by ([3](#S2.E3 "In 2
│ .1 Equations of Motion ‣ 2 Setup ‣ Simple Proof for Global Existence of Bohmia
│ n Trajectories")) or ([5](#S2.E5 "In 2.1 Equations of Motion ‣ 2 Setup ‣ Simpl
│ e Proof for Global Existence of Bohmian Trajectories")) is singular at the nod
│ es (i.e., zeros) of the wavefunction. In particular, there are trajectories th
│ at are not defined for all times because they run into a node. Thus, the stron
│ gest statement one can expect to be true is that global existence holds for *a
│ lmost all* solutions of the equation of motion. As we show, this is in fact tr
│ ue for suitable potentials and initial wavefunctions. As a by-product, one obt
│ ains from almost-sure global existence the equivariance of the |ψt|2|\\psi\_{t
│ }|^{2} distributions.
│
│ The relevance of Bohmian mechanics to the foundations of quantum mechanics ari
│ ses from the fact that a world governed by Bohmian mechanics satisfies all pro
│ bability rules of quantum mechanics \[[5](#bib.bib5 ""), [1](#bib.bib1 ""), [1
│ 1](#bib.bib11 ""), [3](#bib.bib3 ""), [10](#bib.bib10 "")\]. Bohmian mechanics
│  thus provides an example of a “quantum theory without observers,” one in whic
│ h no reference to observers is needed for the formulation of the theory, and a
│ n explanation of the quantum probabilities in terms of objective events.
│
│ The authoritative paper on global existence of Bohmian trajectories is by Bern
│ dl et al. \[[4](#bib.bib4 "")\]; see also \[[2](#bib.bib2 "")\]. We note that
│ the proof given by Holland \[[15](#bib.bib15 ""), p. 85\] is incorrect (see \[
│ [4](#bib.bib4 "")\] for details)
│
│ [Answer truncated]

◆ Wrote research/experiments/bohm-preflight-001/audit/03-flow-equivariance.md (+
154)
    1 +# 03 — Flow, transport, equivariance (local DERIVED; global SOURCED)
    2 +
    3 +Parents: doc 01 (freeze, B-register), doc 02 (continuity lemma (C)).
    4 +Proves the frozen pushforward statement locally from stated premises;
    5 +global conclusion via one inspected primary source (B7). Local and
    6 +global are established separately, as frozen.
    7 +
    8 +---
    9 +
   10 +## 1. Guidance field and local flow (DERIVED modulo standard ODE)
   11 +
   12 +On the open set \(\{\rho>0\}\), \(v_k = j_k/\rho\) with \(j_k\) from
   13 +doc 02. By B2, \(j_k\) is \(C^1\) and \(\rho\) is \(C^1\) and
   14 +nonvanishing there, so each \(v_k\) is \(C^1\), hence locally
   15 +Lipschitz (quotient rule; DERIVED from calculus). By B6 (standard
   16 +ODE theory, Picard–Lindelöf, SOURCE-REPORTED): for every
   17 +\((t_0,q_0)\) with \(\rho(t_0,q_0)>0\) there exist a maximal open
   18 +interval \(I\ni t_0\) and a unique \(C^1\) solution
   19 +\(t\mapsto Q(t;t_0,q_0)\) of \(\dot q_k = v_k(t,q)\),
   20 +\(Q(t_0)=q_0\), defined as long as the trajectory stays in
   21 +\(\{\rho>0\}\) and bounded. The local flow map
   22 +\(\Phi_{t,t_0}(q_0) = Q(t;t_0,q_0)\) is a \(C^1\) diffeomorphism
   23 +onto its image (standard ODE flow theorem, SOURCE-REPORTED).
   24 +Nothing is asserted about arrival at nodes, blowup, or escape:
   25 +those are the obstructions B7 addresses globally.
   26 +
   27 +## 2. Transport identity (DERIVED)
   28 +
   29 +Let \(\sigma(t,q)\) be \(C^1\) and consider its values along flow
   30 +trajectories: \(\frac{d}{dt}\sigma(t,\Phi_{t,t_0}(q_0))
   31 += \partial_t\sigma + v\!\cdot\!\nabla\sigma\) (chain rule).
   32 +Conservation of \(\sigma\)-mass in comoving volumes is equivalent
   33 +to the Eulerian transport equation
   34 +\[ \partial_t\sigma + \nabla\!\cdot\!(\sigma v) = 0,
   35 +\tag{T} \]
   36 +by the Reynolds transport theorem for \(C^1\) fields (standard
   37 +calculus, DERIVED from the divergence theorem under B4-type decay
   38 +for the integrated form; pointwise form needs no decay).
   39 +KEY STEP: the Schrödinger density \(\rho_t = |\psi_t|^2\) satisfies
   40 +(T) with the guidance \(v\), because \(\rho_t v = j_t\) HOLDS BY
   41 +CONSTRUCTION of \(v=j/\rho\) on \(\{\rho>0\}\), and doc-02 (C)
   42 +reads \(\partial_t\rho + \nabla\!\cdot\! j = 0\). So (C) IS the
   43 +transport equation for \(\rho\) along the Bohmian flow wherever
   44 +the flow is defined. Consumes: doc 02 + S1 definitions. No new
   45 +premise.
   46 +
   47 +## 3. Uniqueness lemma (DERIVED)
   48 +
   49 +Premise U1 (stated): on the time interval of interest, \(v\) is
   50 +\(C^1\) with bounded \(\nabla\!\cdot\! v\), and solutions decay
   51 +enough that boundary flux vanishes (follows from B4 for
   52 +Schrödinger densities).
   53 +Lemma: under U1, (T) with given \(C^1\) initial data \(\sigma_0\)
   54 +has at most one \(C^1\) solution. Proof (energy estimate):
   55 +for \(w=\sigma^1-\sigma^2\) solving \(\partial_t w+\nabla\!\cdot\!(wv)
   56 +=0\) with \(w(0)=0\),
   57 +\(\frac{d}{dt}\int w^2 = 2\int w\,\partial_t w
   58 += -2\int w\,\nabla\!\cdot\!(wv)
   59 += -2\int w(v\!\cdot\!\nabla w + w\,\nabla\!\cdot\! v)
   60 += -\int v\!\cdot\!\nabla(w^2) - 2\int w^2\nabla\!\cdot\! v
   61 += -\int w^2\nabla\!\cdot\! v\)
   62 +(integration by parts; boundary term vanishes by U1-decay).
   63 +Hence \(|\frac{d}{dt}\|w\|_2^2| \le \sup|\nabla\!\cdot\! v|\,
   64 +\|w\|_2^2\) and Grönwall gives \(\|w\|_2\equiv0\).
   65 +Disposition: DERIVED from U1 + calculus. (U1 is the precise
   66 +analytic price of uniqueness; it is NOT theorem-free.)
   67 +
   68 +## 4. Local equivariance (DERIVED)
   69 +
   70 +Theorem (local). Assume B1–B6 + U1 on \([t_0,t_1]\). Then
   71 +\[ \boxed{ (\Phi_{t,t_0})_\#\,\rho_{t_0} = \rho_t,
   72 +   \qquad t\in[t_0,t_1]. } \tag{E-loc} \]
   73 +Proof: \(\sigma_t := (\Phi_{t,t_0})_\#\rho_{t_0}\) (pushforward of
   74 +a density along a \(C^1\) diffeomorphism) satisfies (T) with
   75 +\(\sigma_{t_0}=\rho_{t_0}\) (standard transport-of-density fact:
   76 +change of variables + §2; the pushforward is DEFINED to be
   77 +conserved along the flow). \(\rho_t\) satisfies (T) with the same
   78 +data (§2). By the Lemma (§3) they coincide. ∎
   79 +What made this work: (C) + the DEFINITION \(v=j/\rho\) + local
   80 +flow + uniqueness. The continuity equation alone proves nothing
   81 +about distributions; the flow + uniqueness do the rest. This is
   82 +the deliverable floor: fully derived, no imports beyond standard
   83 +ODE/calculus (B6 + U1-proof ingredients).
   84 +
   85 +## 5. Global equivariance (SOURCE-REPORTED import)
   86 +
   87 +Inspected primary source: Teufel–Tumulka, "Simple Proof for
   88 +Global Existence of Bohmian Trajectories", arXiv:math-ph/0406030
   89 +(CMP 258 (2005) 349–365), full text inspected 2026-11-06:
   90 +Theorem 1 (current-field existence + equivariance on \([0,T]\)
   91 +under integrability conditions (15)–(17): a.s.-no node approach,
   92 +no escape to infinity, no singular-set approach); Theorem 3 +
   93 +Corollary 4 (spinless Schrödinger case: \(V=V_1+V_2\) smooth,
   94 +\(V_1\) bounded below, \(V_2\) \(-\tfrac12\Delta\)-form-bounded
   95 +relative bound <1; \(\psi(0)\in C^\infty(H)\) normalized
   96 +\(\Rightarrow\) trajectories exist globally for
   97 +\(\mu_0\)-almost every \(q\), and \(|\psi_t|^2\) equivariant).
   98 +Imported statement (B7, closed as SOURCE-REPORTED): under the
   99 +TT potential/wavefunction class (which CONTAINS our S1 freeze
  100 +with B3 strengthened to smooth + bounded-below \(V\) — recorded
  101 +B3-final), the Bohmian flow exists for all \(t\) for
  102 +\(\mu_0\)-almost every initial \(q\), and
  103 +\[ \boxed{ (\Phi_{t,t_0})_\#\,\rho_{t_0} = \rho_t
  104 +   \quad\text{for all }t,\ \mu_{t_0}\text{-a.e.} }
  105 +\tag{E-glob} \]
  106 +Qualifications carried verbatim from the source: ALMOST ALL
  107 +(nodes genuinely kill some trajectories in finite time —
  108 +Holland p.85's proof to the contrary is incorrect per TT §1);
  109 +self-adjoint \(H\) with \(\psi(0)\in C^\infty(H)\); singular
  110 +(Coulomb-type) potentials need the excised-configuration
  111 +formulation (outside our freeze; NOT claimed). Our B5 (no node
  112 +assertions) is consistent with the a.e. qualification. The TT
  113 +proof itself is not reproduced; its statement is imported with
  114 +the inspected citation above. B3-final: smooth + bounded-below
  115 +\(V\) (import-grade); singular \(V\) excluded.
  116 +
  117 +## 6. MRC regime/scope + dispositions
  118 +
  119 +ESTABLISHED (this record): system freeze S1; continuity (C)
  120 +DERIVED (doc 02); local flow + transport identity + uniqueness
  121 +lemma + (E-loc) DERIVED modulo standard ODE/calculus (B6, U1
  122 +ingredients); global (E-glob) SOURCE-REPORTED via inspected
  123 +TT with a.e. qualification. REGIME: finite spinless
  124 +nonrelativistic particles, smooth bounded-below potential,
  125 +classical (\(C^2\)) solutions, local-in-time fully derived,
  126 +global a.s. imported. NOT established: node-hit behavior,
  127 +singular potentials, spin/magnetic/relativistic cases (TT cover
  128 +them; we do not import what we do not need), typicality,
  129 +Born-rule justification, measurement, interpretation
  130 +comparison — all excluded per doc 01 §4. MRC debt: none open
  131 +inside the frozen scope; every B-premise is either consumed by
  132 +a derivation step above or explicitly imported (B6/U1-calculus
  133 +standard, B7-TT).
  134 +
  135 +## 7. Fixture handoff (binding on Step 4)
  136 +
  137 +MAY PIN (executable on explicit test inputs via DIFFERENTIATE-001
  138 ++ CANON/SIMPLIFY, minisuperspace precedent): current-assembly
  139 +identities (\(j\) from \(\psi^*\nabla\psi\) parts, both §1 forms);
  140 +continuity-residual computation on test wavefunctions (solution
  141 +test input → residual zero; NON-solution test input → residual
  142 +nonzero as an honest NEGATIVE check); guidance quotient
  143 +construction with NEQ(\(\rho\),0) conditions (node-blind assembly
  144 +without the condition is the ANTI-GOLDEN); coefficient identities
  145 +(\(i\cdot i=-1\) chain, \(\hbar^2/\hbar\) chain as rational
  146 +skeletons). MUST SKETCH/limit (unexecutable: no integration rule,
  147 +no ODE/flow execution, no measure/pushforward, PDE existence
  148 +unrepresentable): flow ODE existence, pushforward definition,
  149 +uniqueness lemma, global existence, node analysis, (E-loc)/(E-glob)

│ // Package equivariance is the 1D single-particle Bohmian continuity,
│ // current, and guidance evidence fixture: executed fragments of the
│ // doc-02/doc-03 chain (audit/02-continuity-derivation.md,
│ // audit/03-flow-equivariance.md) with replayed DIFFERENTIATE/SIMPLIFY
│ // evidence.
│ //
│ // Status discipline (see manifest.json): on explicit test wave-function
│ // pairs in test units (hbar = m = 1, test input only, never claimed
│ // solutions of the Schrödinger equation) the current assembly,
│ // continuity residual (solution pair → zero; non-solution pair →
│ // pinned nonzero form), product-rule expansion identity, and guidance
│ // quotient (with recorded NEQ(rho,0) conditions) are EXECUTED with
│ // replayed evidence. Flow ODE existence, pushforward/measure transport,
│ // the uniqueness lemma, global existence, node behavior, and the
│ // equivariance theorems (E-loc)/(E-glob) are REPRESENTED relations or
│ // UNSUPPORTED as recorded — never claimed executed. This is a
│ // symmetry-reduced 1D fragment, not proof of the general theory.
│ package equivariance

│ // Fixture holds every minted artifact of the equivariance evidence chain.
│ type Fixture struct {
│     Store    physmath.Store
│     ChartRef artifact.ArtifactRef // coordinate_system/1 1D line chart
│     // Solution pair: R1 = 1, S1 = 2q (satisfies continuity).
│     R1FnRef, R1Ref artifact.ArtifactRef
│     S1FnRef, S1Ref artifact.ArtifactRef
│     Rho1Ref       artifact.ArtifactRef // expression/1 rho1 = R1^2
│     DtRho1Ref     artifact.ArtifactRef // expression/1 d_t rho1
│     DqS1Ref       artifact.ArtifactRef // expression/1 d_q S1
│     J1Ref         artifact.ArtifactRef // expression/1 j1 = rho1 d_q S1
│     DqJ1Ref       artifact.ArtifactRef // expression/1 d_q j1
│     Res1Ref       artifact.ArtifactRef // expression/1 residual d_t rho1 + d_q
│  j1
│     Res1ZeroRef   artifact.ArtifactRef // relation/1 EQ(Res1, 0)
│     V1Ref         artifact.ArtifactRef // expression/1 v1 = j1 rho1^-1
│     Guide1RelRef  artifact.ArtifactRef // relation/1 EQ(v1, j1 rho1^-1)
│     // Non-solution pair: R2 = t^2+q^2+1, S2 = t q.
│     R2FnRef, R2Ref artifact.ArtifactRef
│     S2FnRef, S2Ref artifact.ArtifactRef
│     Rho2Ref       artifact.ArtifactRef
│     DtRho2Ref     artifact.ArtifactRef
│     DqS2Ref       artifact.ArtifactRef
│     J2Ref         artifact.ArtifactRef
│     DqJ2Ref       artifact.ArtifactRef
│     Res2Ref       artifact.ArtifactRef
│     PRRelRef      artifact.ArtifactRef // relation/1 EQ(executed d_q j2,
│ manual product-rule assembly)
│     V2Ref         artifact.ArtifactRef
│     Guide2RelRef  artifact.ArtifactRef
│     // Node pair: R3 = q, S3 = q (rho3 vanishes at q = 0).
│     R3FnRef, R3Ref artifact.ArtifactRef
│     S3FnRef, S3Ref artifact.ArtifactRef
│     Rho3Ref       artifact.ArtifactRef
│     J3Ref         artifact.ArtifactRef
│     V3Ref         artifact.ArtifactRef
│     Guide3RelRef  artifact.ArtifactRef
│     // Represented-only claim relations (no derivation targets them).
│     FlowRelRef  artifact.ArtifactRef // relation/1 flow ODE (LHS represented)
│     PushRelRef  artifact.ArtifactRef // relation/1 pushforward transport
│     EquivRelRef artifact.ArtifactRef // relation/1 (E-loc) claim
│     Derivs      []artifact.ArtifactRef
│     Symbols     map[string]artifact.ArtifactRef
│     Conds       map[string]artifact.ArtifactRef
│ }
│
│ func sym(s physmath.Store, name string) artifact.ArtifactRef {
│     ref, err := physmath.PutExpression(s, physmath.NewEphemeralSymbol(name))
│     if err != nil {
│         panic(err)
│     }
│     return ref
│ }
│
│ func condNEQ(s physmath.Store, e physmath.Expr) artifact.ArtifactRef {
│     body, err := physmath.EncodeRelationBody(physmath.OpNEQ, e,
│ physmath.MustRational(0, 1))
│     if err != nil {
│         panic(err)
│     }
│     rel, err := s.Put(physmath.FamilyRelation, body)
│     if err != nil {
│         panic(err)
│     }
│     cond, err := s.Put(physmath.FamilyCondition, rel.Encode())
│     if err != nil {
│         panic(err)
│     }
│     return cond
│ }
│
│ func diffParams(fn artifact.ArtifactRef, pid physmath.ParameterID, order
│ uint32) []byte {
│     return append(physmath.BoundParameterRef{DeclaringArtifactRef: fn,
│ ParameterID: pid}.Encode(),
│         artifact.EncodeUInt32(order)...)
│ }
│
│ func mintFn(s physmath.Store, ids []physmath.ParameterID, names []string, body
│  physmath.Expr) artifact.ArtifactRef {
│     bodyRef, err := physmath.PutExpression(s, body)
│     if err != nil {
│         panic(err)
│     }
│     fn := physmath.Function{ParameterIDs: ids, ParamNames: names, BodyRef:
│ bodyRef}
│     if err := physmath.ValidateFunctionBody(fn, body); err != nil {
│         panic(err)
│     }
│     payload, err := fn.Encode()
│     if err != nil {
│         panic(err)
│     }
│     ref, err := s.Put(physmath.FamilyFunction, payload)
│     if err != nil {
│         panic(err)
│     }
│     return ref
│ }
│
│ func refOf(s physmath.Store, fnRef artifact.ArtifactRef)
│ (artifact.ArtifactRef, error) {
│     fam, payload, err := s.Lookup(fnRef)
│     if err != nil {
│         return artifact.ArtifactRef{}, err
│     }
│     if fam != physmath.FamilyFunction {
│         return artifact.ArtifactRef{}, fmt.Errorf("not function/1")
│     }
│     fn, err := physmath.DecodeFunction(artifact.NewDecoder(payload))
│     if err != nil {
│         return artifact.ArtifactRef{}, err
│     }
│     return fn.BodyRef, nil
│ }
│
│ func mustResolve(s physmath.Store, ref artifact.ArtifactRef) physmath.Expr {
│     fam, payload, err := s.Lookup(ref)
│     if err != nil {
│         panic(err)
│     }
│     if fam != physmath.FamilyExpression {
│         panic("not expression/1")
│     }
│     e, err := physmath.DecodeExpr(artifact.NewDecoder(payload))
│     if err != nil {
│         panic(err)
│     }
│     return e
│ }
│
│ func diffReplay(s physmath.Store, in, fn artifact.ArtifactRef, pid
│ physmath.ParameterID, order uint32, conds []artifact.ArtifactRef)
│ (artifact.ArtifactRef, artifact.ArtifactRef) {
│     res, err := physmath.Apply(s, physmath.Invocation{
│         ProducerNamespace: physmath.NamespacePhysMath, RuleID:
│ physmath.RuleDifferentiate,
│         InputRefs: []artifact.ArtifactRef{in, fn}, ConditionRefs: conds,
│         Parameters: diffParams(fn, pid, order),
│     })
│     if err != nil {
│         panic(err)
│     }
│     d := physmath.Derivation{
│         PremiseRefs: []artifact.ArtifactRef{in, fn},
│         Steps: []physmath.DerivationStep{{
│             Ordinal: 0, ProducerNamespace: physmath.NamespacePhysMath, RuleID:
│  physmath.RuleDifferentiate,
│             InputRefs: []artifact.ArtifactRef{in, fn}, OutputRef:
│ res.OutputRef,
│             ConditionRefs: res.ConditionRefs, Parameters: diffParams(fn, pid,
│ order),
│         }},
│         ResultRefs: []artifact.ArtifactRef{res.OutputRef},
│     }
│     if err := physmath.Replay(s, d, nil); err != nil {
│         panic(err)
│     }
│     return res.OutputRef, mustMintDerivation(s, d)
│ }
│
│ func simpReplay(s physmath.Store, in artifact.ArtifactRef, conds
│ []artifact.ArtifactRef) (artifact.ArtifactRef, artifact.ArtifactRef) {
│     res, err := physmath.Apply(s, physmath.Invocation{
│         ProducerNamespace: physmath.NamespacePhysMath, RuleID:
│ physmath.RuleExprSimplify,
│         InputRefs: []artifact.ArtifactRef{in}, ConditionRefs: conds,
│     })
│     if err != nil {
│         panic(err)
│     }
│     d := physmath.Derivation{
│         PremiseRefs: []artifact.ArtifactRef{in},
│         Steps: []physmath.DerivationStep{{
│             Ordinal: 0, ProducerNamespace: physmath.NamespacePhysMath, RuleID:
│  physmath.RuleExprSimplify,
│             InputRefs: []artifact.ArtifactRef{in}, OutputRef: res.OutputRef,
│             ConditionRefs: res.ConditionRefs,
│         }},
│         ResultRefs: []artifact.ArtifactRef{res.OutputRef},
│     }
│     if err := physmath.Replay(s, d, nil); err != nil {
│         panic(err)
│     }
│     return res.OutputRef, mustMintDerivation(s, d)
│ }
│
│ func mustMintDerivation(s physmath.Store, d physmath.Derivation)
│ artifact.ArtifactRef {
│     raw, err := d.Encode()
│     if err != nil {
│         panic(err)
│     }
│     ref, err := s.Put(physmath.FamilyDerivation, raw)
│     if err != nil {
│         panic(err)
│     }
│     return ref
│ }
│
│ func spaceCollection(s physmath.Store, name string) artifact.ArtifactRef {
│     desig, err := physmath.PutExpression(s, physmath.NewEphemeralSymbol(name))
│     if err != nil {
│         panic(err)
│     }
│     raw, err := physmath.Collection{ElementRefs:
│ []artifact.ArtifactRef{desig}}.Encode()
│     if err != nil {
│         panic(err)
│     }
│     ref, err := s.Put(physmath.FamilyCollection, raw)
│     if err != nil {
│         panic(err)
│     }
│     return ref
│ }
│
│ func putExpr(s physmath.Store, e physmath.Expr) artifact.ArtifactRef {
│     ref, err := physmath.PutExpression(s, e)
│     if err != nil {
│         panic(err)
│     }
│     return ref
│ }
│
│ func putRelation(s physmath.Store, op physmath.ComparisonOp, left, right
│ physmath.Expr) artifact.ArtifactRef {
│     body, err := physmath.EncodeRelationBody(op, left, right)
│     if err != nil {
│         panic(err)
│     }
│     ref, err := s.Put(physmath.FamilyRelation, body)
│     if err != nil {
│         panic(err)
│     }
│     return ref
│ }
